SYNTHETIC REWARD LARGE-BATCH CONTROL
Experiment: grpo-mbpp-synthetic-direction-probe-g16x8
Purpose: Test whether eight independent reward groups per optimizer update and a constant learning rate stabilize the GRPO update direction.
Timestamp: 2026-09-20 UTC
Git commit: bf7b7da
W&B project: kunal-personal/grpo-mbpp
W&B run: y59wjl8b
Candidate artifact: outputs/grpo-mbpp-synthetic-direction-probe-g16x8/final
Selection procedure: No candidate selection was performed. This was an optimizer control experiment.
Data: Eight fixed disjoint MBPP training tasks, with no held-out benchmark claim.
Configuration: Qwen/Qwen2.5-Coder-3B-Instruct, LoRA rank 16, bf16, constant learning rate 3e-6, four optimizer updates, 128 sampled completions per update, eight groups of 16 completions, one-sample microbatches, gradient accumulation 128, temperature 1.0, maximum completion length 2048, and beta 0.
Metrics:
  - 512 synthetic reward traces were recorded, with every update containing eight mixed groups and synthetic reward mean 0.5.
  - Advantage to log-probability delta correlation by update was [0.004, -0.120, -0.035, 0.130].
  - Positive-advantage mean log-probability delta was [-0.000062, -0.000507, -0.000116, 0.000238].
  - Negative-advantage mean log-probability delta was [-0.000075, 0.000031, 0.000021, -0.000368].
  - Advantage sign alignment was [0.492, 0.492, 0.547, 0.602].
  - Parameter update norms were [0.011750, 0.008558, 0.007262, 0.006528], with cumulative LoRA movement 0.027990 at the end.
Interpretation: Increasing the effective batch from 16 to 128 and removing learning-rate decay did not produce consistent synthetic reward direction. This weakens the hypothesis that the earlier result was only small-batch variance. The next diagnostic should be a deterministic two-completion gradient test that verifies reward ordering, advantage signs, token masks, and loss sign directly.
Limitation: Final merged-model vLLM evaluation failed because only 2.41 GiB of GPU memory was free and vLLM required 14.02 GiB at its configured utilization. This control has no benchmark result.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

SYNTHETIC REWARD DIRECTION CONTROL
Experiment: grpo-mbpp-synthetic-direction-probe-g16
Purpose: Test GRPO update direction with a known reward ranking independent of Python test scoring.
Timestamp: 2026-09-20 UTC
Git commit: 1954b90
W&B project: kunal-personal/grpo-mbpp
W&B run: tfx5zuj9
Candidate artifact: outputs/grpo-mbpp-synthetic-direction-probe-g16/final
Selection procedure: No candidate selection was performed. This was an optimizer control experiment.
Data: Eight fixed disjoint MBPP training tasks, with no held-out benchmark claim.
Configuration: Qwen/Qwen2.5-Coder-3B-Instruct, LoRA rank 16, bf16, learning rate 3e-6, four optimizer updates, 16 sampled completions per task group, one-sample microbatches, gradient accumulation 16, temperature 1.0, maximum completion length 2048, beta 0, and synthetic_reward_probe enabled.
Synthetic reward: Within each 16-completion group, indices 0 through 7 received reward 1 and indices 8 through 15 received reward 0. The original sandbox score was retained as execution_reward in the trace.
Trace artifact: outputs/grpo-mbpp-synthetic-direction-probe-g16/reward-trace.jsonl
Metrics:
  - 64 completion traces were recorded. Synthetic rewards were 32 ones and 32 zeros.
  - W&B rewards/reward/mean was 0.5 on every update and reward/group_count was 1 with mixed_group_fraction 1.
  - Advantage to log-probability delta correlation by update was [0.0362, 0.1246, 0.4761, 0.1300].
  - Positive-advantage mean log-probability delta was [-0.000198, 0.000460, 0.000367, -0.000164].
  - Negative-advantage mean log-probability delta was [-0.000339, -0.000366, -0.000364, -0.000442].
  - Advantage sign alignment was [0.500, 0.563, 0.813, 0.500].
  - Parameter update norms were [0.011856, 0.006866, 0.003642, 0.001704].
Interpretation: The known reward produced the expected update direction strongly on update 3, but the four-update run was not consistently aligned. This weakens the claim that the earlier negative result was solely caused by test-reward noise, while leaving shared-parameter interference, small group count, and the decaying learning rate as live explanations.
Limitation: Final merged-model vLLM evaluation did not run because only 6.46 GiB of 15.58 GiB GPU memory was free and vLLM required 14.02 GiB at its configured utilization. This control has no held-out evaluation result.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## Experiment: reward scoring and update direction probe, v4

Status: completed GRPO updates; final post-training reload failed from CUDA out of memory after the step-eight evaluation artifact was written.

Purpose: verify the hybrid reward on exact sampled code and test whether one GRPO update increases the likelihood of higher-advantage completions.

Setup: Qwen/Qwen2.5-Coder-3B-Instruct with LoRA, eight fixed disjoint MBPP training tasks, 16 sampled completions per rollout, eight optimizer steps, one rollout group per optimizer update using 16 microbatches, learning rate 3e-6 with a linear schedule, bfloat16, 2048 maximum completion tokens, hybrid reward 0.5 times full pass plus 0.5 times original MBPP test fraction, and greedy vLLM evaluation on the same eight tasks. The canonical 399 task EvalPlus evaluation was disabled. Candidate selection used only the tiny training-set pass@1.

Artifacts: W&B project kunal-personal/grpo-mbpp, run grpo-mbpp-reward-direction-probe-g16-v4, ID i5sozc3j; output outputs/grpo-mbpp-reward-direction-probe-g16-v4; reward trace reward-trace.jsonl; code commit a751b25.

Reward scoring result: 128 sampled completions were traced. The reward trace preserved task IDs, raw and truncated completions, scored code, test code, status, passed and total tests, component rewards, and scalar reward. Status counts were 68 full passes, 21 partial completions, 38 failed executions, and 1 syntax error. Every task received 16 samples. Per-task mean reward was 0.531 for task 601, 0.052 for 609, 0.875 for 613, 0.073 for 617, 0.875 for 621, 0.875 for 625, 0.417 for 627, and 0.812 for 634.

Update direction result: pass@1 stayed at 0.625 and overall test fraction stayed at 0.75 from step zero to step eight. Across eight updates, advantage to sequence-log-probability-delta correlations averaged -0.055. Mean log-probability change was -0.000182 for positive-advantage samples and +0.000133 for negative-advantage samples. Sign alignment averaged 0.461, close to chance. This indicates that the updates did not consistently reinforce higher-advantage samples.

Training reward result: rollout reward means were 0.417, 0.073, 0.531, 0.813, 0.875, 0.052, 0.875, and 0.875. The final cumulative average was 0.564, but this was driven by large task-to-task variation rather than a consistent monotonic policy improvement. The reward trace and direction probe jointly support the conclusion that the scorer is functioning and the update direction is inefficient or misaligned.

Limitations: the direction probe measures sequence-level log-probability changes after each aggregate update and does not prove that every token-level gradient is correct. The last final evaluation reload failed because the training model still occupied nearly all GPU memory, but step-eight evaluation metrics were written before that failure. The experiment has eight prompts and eight updates, so it diagnoses the mechanism rather than estimating generalization.

EXPERIMENT RESULTS
Experiment: llm-output-verifier-evalplus-20-2026-09-11
Purpose: Test whether one additional LLM call can select the correct program from ten already generated EvalPlus candidates.
Model: gpt-5-mini through the OpenAI API.
Tasks: The first 20 tasks in the saved official 399-task EvalPlus artifact.
Candidate artifact: outputs/qwen-evalplus-full-10-temp02/mbpp/qwen2_chat_temp_0.2/eval_results.json.
Observed-output artifact: outputs/qwen-evalplus-full-10-temp02/output-clusters-v3/observations.
Verifier input: The original task prompt, entry point, all standard and extended test inputs, every saved candidate program, and every saved returned value or runtime error.
Selection procedure: One verifier call per task selected one candidate index. Five concurrent requests were used after two earlier sequential requests had completed. Correctness labels were withheld from all verifier prompts and joined only for measurement.
Reusable decision artifact: outputs/qwen-evalplus-full-10-temp02/llm-output-verifier-20.json.
Git commit for runner: e41c947.

Matched 20-task results:
{
  "verifier": {
    "base_accuracy": 0.55,
    "plus_accuracy": 0.5,
    "combined_accuracy": 0.5,
    "base_correct": 11,
    "plus_correct": 10,
    "combined_correct": 10,
    "tasks": 20
  },
  "baseline_prefix_pass_at_k": {
    "base": {
      "pass_at_1": 0.55,
      "pass_at_2": 0.55,
      "pass_at_4": 0.55,
      "pass_at_8": 0.55,
      "pass_at_10": 0.55
    },
    "plus": {
      "pass_at_1": 0.5,
      "pass_at_2": 0.5,
      "pass_at_4": 0.5,
      "pass_at_8": 0.5,
      "pass_at_10": 0.5
    },
    "combined": {
      "pass_at_1": 0.5,
      "pass_at_2": 0.5,
      "pass_at_4": 0.5,
      "pass_at_8": 0.5,
      "pass_at_10": 0.5
    }
  }
}

Interpretation: On this 20-task slice, the LLM verifier matched the ordinary candidate-prefix envelope at every reported K because the tasks had no additional correct candidates after the first successful candidate or had no successful candidate. This small slice shows no measurable improvement from the verifier.

EXPERIMENT: llm-standard-test-answer-preview-2026-09-11
Purpose: Preview a method that asks gpt-5-mini to predict the outputs of only the three standard MBPP tests, then identifies saved candidates with matching outputs.
Tasks: Mbpp/782 and Mbpp/775.
Prompt input: Task statement, entry point, and three standard MBPP test inputs. Extended tests and correctness labels were withheld.
Results:
{
  "Mbpp/782": {
    "predicted_outputs": [14, 15, 8],
    "matching_candidate_indices": []
  },
  "Mbpp/775": {
    "predicted_outputs": [true, true, false],
    "matching_candidate_indices": [0, 5, 6]
  }
}
Artifact: outputs/qwen-evalplus-full-10-temp02/llm-test-answer-preview.json.
Interpretation: This preview is invalid for measuring independent answer prediction. The raw task prompt included the visible assertion with answer 14, and the original Qwen generation path also passed that raw prompt through. GPT-5 mini predicted all three outputs correctly for Mbpp/782, but the first output was leaked in the prompt.

EXPERIMENT: llm-standard-test-answer-prediction-evalplus-399-2026-09-11
Purpose: Measure GPT-5 mini accuracy when predicting the outputs of the stored standard MBPP tests for every official EvalPlus task.
Model: gpt-5-mini through the OpenAI API.
Tasks: All 399 tasks in the saved official EvalPlus artifact.
Prompt condition: The raw MBPP task prompt, including its visible assertion examples, plus all stored base inputs. This matches the prompt condition used for the saved Qwen candidates.
Concurrency: Started at 32 workers and completed without rate limiting or reduction.
Ground truth: Each task's trusted canonical solution was executed on every stored base input after prediction. Canonical outputs that were not JSON-like were retained as unsupported typed values and counted incorrect unless the prediction matched the supported comparison.
Prediction artifact: outputs/qwen-evalplus-full-10-temp02/llm-test-answer-predictions-399.json.
Runner commit: e3335e2.

Metrics:
{
  "standard_test_output_accuracy": 0.8826860841,
  "correct_outputs": 1091,
  "total_outputs": 1236,
  "all_standard_tests_correct_task_accuracy": 0.8571428571,
  "tasks_with_all_outputs_correct": 342,
  "tasks": 399,
  "tasks_with_at_least_one_matching_saved_candidate": 231,
  "matching_saved_candidate_count": 1772
}

Limitations: The raw MBPP prompt contains visible assertion examples, so this measures GPT-5 mini under the same information condition as the original candidate generations. Thirty tasks contain more than three stored base inputs, giving 1,236 total standard inputs. Eight canonical outputs across four tasks used unsupported runtime types.

EXPERIMENT: llm-prediction-reranking-evalplus-399-2026-09-11
Purpose: Rank each task's ten saved candidates by the fraction of standard-test outputs matching cached GPT-5 mini predictions, then measure Pass@K after ranking.
Selection procedure: Candidate ranking used only GPT prediction agreement and saved candidate execution outputs. Ties preserved original candidate index order. EvalPlus correctness labels were joined only after ranking.
Ranking artifact: outputs/qwen-evalplus-full-10-temp02/llm-prediction-reranking-399.json.
Runner commit: 4af2dc8.

Reranked versus original-order Pass@K:
| K | Base reranked | Base original | MBPP+ reranked | MBPP+ original | Combined reranked | Combined original |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 1 | 63.91% | 51.38% | 51.63% | 43.61% | 50.63% | 42.86% |
| 2 | 64.91% | 56.89% | 53.88% | 47.87% | 53.13% | 46.87% |
| 3 | 65.66% | 61.15% | 55.64% | 51.38% | 54.64% | 50.38% |
| 4 | 65.66% | 61.65% | 55.89% | 52.38% | 54.89% | 51.38% |
| 5 | 65.91% | 62.66% | 55.89% | 53.38% | 54.89% | 52.38% |
| 6 | 65.91% | 63.41% | 55.89% | 53.88% | 54.89% | 52.88% |
| 7 | 66.17% | 64.66% | 56.14% | 54.64% | 55.14% | 53.63% |
| 8 | 66.17% | 65.41% | 56.64% | 55.64% | 55.64% | 54.64% |
| 9 | 66.42% | 65.66% | 56.89% | 56.14% | 55.89% | 55.14% |
| 10 | 66.42% | 66.42% | 56.89% | 56.89% | 55.89% | 55.89% |

The largest absolute gain is at Pass@1: +12.53 percentage points on base, +8.02 points on MBPP+, and +7.77 points on combined correctness. Pass@10 is unchanged because reranking preserves the same ten-candidate pool.

EXPERIMENT: answer-prediction-correctness-cohorts-2026-09-11
Purpose: Measure GPT-5 mini standard-test answer prediction separately for tasks where the ten saved candidates are mixed, all correct, or all incorrect on the base tests.
Grouping: A task is all-correct when all ten saved candidates pass every base test, all-incorrect when none pass every base test, and mixed otherwise. Grouping uses saved correctness only for post-prediction analysis.
Artifact: outputs/qwen-evalplus-full-10-temp02/answer-prediction-cohorts.json.

| Candidate cohort | Tasks | Correct outputs | Total outputs | Per-test accuracy | Whole-task accuracy |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| Mixed | 125 | 347 | 385 | 90.13% | 89.60% |
| All ten correct | 140 | 391 | 436 | 89.68% | 88.57% |
| All ten incorrect | 134 | 353 | 415 | 85.06% | 79.10% |

The all-incorrect cohort is harder for GPT-5 mini by 5.07 percentage points per test and 9.50 percentage points per whole task relative to the mixed cohort.

EXPERIMENT: qwen25-coder-3b-answer-prediction-correctness-cohorts-2026-09-11
Purpose: Measure Qwen2.5-Coder-3B-Instruct standard-test answer prediction separately for tasks where the ten saved candidates are mixed, all correct, or all incorrect on the base tests.
Prediction artifact: outputs/qwen-answer-predictions/qwen25-coder-3b.json.
Analysis artifact: outputs/qwen-answer-predictions/qwen25-coder-3b-cohorts.json.

| Candidate cohort | Tasks | Correct outputs | Total outputs | Per-test accuracy | Whole-task accuracy |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| Mixed | 125 | 212 | 385 | 55.06% | 32.00% |
| All ten correct | 140 | 310 | 436 | 71.10% | 57.14% |
| All ten incorrect | 134 | 196 | 415 | 47.23% | 19.40% |

The Qwen 3B model is 23.87 percentage points more accurate per test on all-correct candidate tasks than on all-incorrect candidate tasks.

Experiment: saved-candidate-reranking-audit-2026-09-10
Timestamp: 2026-09-10T00:00:00Z
Purpose: Assess whether the requested filtering and reranking curves can be reproduced without new sandbox execution or ground-truth-based candidate selection.
Candidate artifact: outputs/qwen-evalplus-full-10-temp02/mbpp/qwen2_chat_temp_0.2/eval_results.json
Candidate count: 399 tasks x 10 generations = 3990 candidates
Selection rule: Candidate selection must use answer-independent execution evidence or saved model scores. Ground-truth correctness labels are reserved for post-selection measurement.
Execution policy: No sandbox execution was run for this experiment.

Baseline metrics from saved official EvalPlus pass_at_k.json:
{
  "pass_at_1": 0.5142857143,
  "pass_at_2": 0.5736563631,
  "pass_at_3": 0.6028822055,
  "pass_at_4": 0.6211958468,
  "pass_at_5": 0.6338564666,

Requested rerankers:
- MBPP execution filter: unavailable. The saved artifact has base correctness labels and failing inputs, but no per-test runtime outcome distinguishing exceptions from wrong outputs.
- MBPP+ execution filter: unavailable for the same reason.
- MBPP+ filter with MBPP fallback: unavailable because the required execution-only survivor sets are absent.
- MBPP+ filter with first-generation fallback: unavailable because the required execution-only survivor sets are absent.
- MBPP filter with first-generation fallback: unavailable because the required execution-only survivor sets are absent.
- Maximum execution-coverage selection: unavailable because per-candidate executed-test counts are absent.
- Mean sequence log-likelihood selection: unavailable because no saved scores for these 3990 candidates were found.

Reproducibility metadata:
- Dataset: EvalPlus MBPP, 399 tasks.
- Generations: 10 per task, temperature 0.2 artifact.
- Saved outcome schema: task_id, solution, base_status, plus_status, base_fail_tests, plus_fail_tests.
- Available baseline source: outputs/qwen-evalplus-full-10-temp02/pass_at_k.json.
- Repository commit at audit: 08d1113.
- Limitation: Reproducing the requested answer-independent rerankers requires a persisted per-test runtime-status artifact and a persisted per-candidate mean-log-likelihood artifact, or a new scoring run.


EXPERIMENT RESULTS
Experiment: saved-execution-reranking-2026-09-10
Purpose: Evaluate answer-independent filtering and reranking strategies using one persisted sandbox scan of the existing ten generations per EvalPlus MBPP task.
Repository commit: 94729de
Candidate source: outputs/qwen-evalplus-full-10-temp02/mbpp/qwen2_chat_temp_0.2
Task input source: qwen_eval/eval_plus/MbppPlus-v0.1.0.jsonl
Correctness measurement source: outputs/qwen-evalplus-full-10-temp02/mbpp/qwen2_chat_temp_0.2/eval_results.json
Candidates: 3990 across 399 tasks.
Input calls: 417626. Returned calls: 378412.
Process statuses: {"completed": 3847, "process_error": 49, "process_timeout": 94}
Execution protocol: Each candidate ran in an isolated Python -I subprocess with a three-second wall-clock timeout. Each stored base_input and plus_input case was called with its arguments. A call counted as executed only when the function returned. Return values were ignored. Exceptions, timeouts, syntax failures, and missing entry points were retained as non-executed outcomes.
Selection protocol: MBPP filter keeps candidates with all standard inputs returning. MBPP+ filter keeps candidates with all extended inputs returning. plus_then_mbpp falls back to the MBPP pool when the MBPP+ pool is empty. plus_then_first and mbpp_then_first fall back to generation zero when their pool is empty. max_execution ranks by fraction of all stored inputs returning. Random tie breaks use seed 42 combined with task ID. Saved correctness labels are used only after ordering to compute empirical prefix Pass@K.
Mean sequence log likelihood: omitted as requested.
Artifact: outputs/qwen-evalplus-full-10-temp02/execution_results.jsonl

These are expected accuracies for selecting one candidate. They are not Pass@1 through Pass@10. The all-task values were recomputed from outputs/qwen-evalplus-full-10-temp02/output-clusters-v3/analysis.json after capturing all 399 tasks and 3,990 candidates.


ITERATIVE VERIFIER SEARCH FINAL RESULT
Search: 100 predeclared output-only verifier iterations on 112 mixed combined-MBPP+ tasks.
Stopping condition: Reached iteration 100. The >95% accuracy condition was not reached.
Baseline random selection: 52.50%.
Best strategy: Per-input modal-output agreement, selecting candidate(s) matching the modal observed value on the most inputs.
Best accuracy: 65.18%, an absolute improvement of 12.68 percentage points over random selection and 1.04 percentage points over exact largest-output-cluster selection at 64.14%.
Selection used no correctness labels. Correctness was joined afterward for measurement only. The best rule had ties on 108 of 112 tasks, so its score is the expected accuracy over tied candidates.
Artifact: outputs/qwen-evalplus-full-10-temp02/output-clusters-v3/iterative_verifier_results.json
Search history: logs/hillclimbing.txt

# Output clustering diagnostic

Clusters are formed from exact typed returned values across all standard and extended inputs. No expected answers are provided to candidates or used in cluster construction. Saved base and combined correctness labels are joined afterward.

These are diagnostic mixed-task subsets, not full-benchmark results. Labels identify which tasks to inspect and score the already-fixed selection heuristics.

## Examples where all ten candidates return

### Mbpp/96

Write a python function to find the number of divisors of a given integer.

Cluster members use zero-based evaluation-record indices: [[0, 2, 4, 5, 6, 8], [1, 3, 7, 9]].
Correct candidate counts in those clusters: [6, 0].

Returned values in cluster order: `["[\"int\",4]", "[\"int\",3]"]`.

Write a python function to find the first digit of a given number.

Cluster members use zero-based evaluation-record indices: [[4, 5, 7, 8], [0, 9], [1, 2], [3, 6]].
Correct candidate counts in those clusters: [4, 0, 0, 0].

Example input (base_input, index 0): `[123]`.
Returned values in cluster order: `["[\"int\",1]", "[\"int\",3]", "[\"int\",12]", "[\"str\",\"1\"]"]`.

### Mbpp/775

Write a python function to check whether every odd index contains odd numbers of a given list.

Cluster members use zero-based evaluation-record indices: [[1, 2, 3, 4, 7, 8, 9], [0, 5, 6]].
Correct candidate counts in those clusters: [0, 3].

Example input (base_input, index 0): `[[2, 1, 4, 3, 6, 7, 6, 3]]`.
Returned values in cluster order: `["[\"bool\",false]", "[\"bool\",true]"]`.

## Setup and limitations

{
  "python": "3.12.3 (main, Aug 14 2025, 17:47:21) [GCC 13.3.0]",
  "platform": "Linux-6.8.0-64-generic-x86_64-with-glibc2.39",
  "workers": 8,
  "per_test_timeout_seconds": 1,
  "per_candidate_timeout_seconds": 30,
  "memory_limit_mb": 512,
  "fresh_namespace_per_test": true,
  "input_protocol": "Upstream EvalPlus MBPP input type restoration applied before passing positional arguments. No reference code, assertions, expected outputs, or contract execution.",
  "normalization": "Exact typed values, ordered lists/tuples, sorted dicts and sets, exact float hexadecimal encoding. Unsupported serialization excluded from full-output clusters.",
  "tie_policy": "Exact expected accuracy under uniform choice of tied largest clusters, then uniform member. Consensus ties uniform among candidates. Empty pools fall back to all ten.",
  "diagnostic_only": "Tasks selected by mixed saved labels. Heuristics fixed without labels. Subset accuracy is not full benchmark performance.",
  "deserializer_sha256": "ecee43420dff4708f1fcc2b9f80bc2ea3e159a4ef64b5ba4043650c3c9f1224b",
  "data_sha256": "a855b9de667baea8e2f8aa61f898767a7336872208a85222b35622545e819fe9",
  "evaluation_sha256": "ec28bcb932c5f1668c0f7d54f103037e170e17068f4c7589a8427ce8c8412aeb",
  "script_sha256": "10059fe1dc660e2ff991352454a22896712ef49c8d68422f6e4624bf35c1ecfd",
  "commit": "3baf4a8fad853e5fe5fc8a51813c51b38d2d6eeb"
}

Input types are restored with the vendored upstream EvalPlus deserializer. Typed equality is deliberately stricter than task-specific evaluator comparisons such as set equivalence or approximate floats. Each test reinitializes candidate globals. One second is allowed per test, with 30-second child batches and continuation of only unrecorded tests. Outputs over 4096 characters are shown as previews, but clusters use SHA256 of their complete normalized values. Errors never form output clusters. Repeated generations count as separate votes, so correlated wrong solutions can dominate. Unknown output types are excluded from full-output clusters.

Artifact files include exact candidate code and raw inputs in jobs.jsonl, per-test status/value/hash in observations/, selection membership and joined labels in analysis.json, and file hashes in manifest.json.

The earlier saved execution reranking table is invalid because it joined generation files and correctness records by different orders. Its single random tie draw was also process-dependent, and it omitted EvalPlus input-type restoration. This report executes evaluation-record code directly and verifies its SHA256 before joining labels.


EXPERIMENT: iterative-output-verifier-search

## Qwen 3B answer-prediction reranking on EvalPlus MBPP

Experiment: `qwen3b-prediction-reranking-399`.

Purpose: Reproduce the GPT-5 mini answer-prediction reranker using the saved deterministic Qwen2.5-Coder-3B-Instruct standard-test output predictions, without generating new code candidates or using ground-truth labels during selection.

Setup: The 399 EvalPlus MBPP tasks each retain the original ten candidate programs and their saved standard-test output observations. For each task, candidates were ranked by the number of standard-test outputs matching Qwen 3B's saved predictions. Ties were broken by original candidate index. Ground-truth base, MBPP+, and combined correctness labels were joined only after ranking to compute Pass@K. The Qwen prediction artifact contains 1,236 standard-test predictions and was generated locally with deterministic decoding.

Artifacts:
- Predictions: `outputs/qwen-answer-predictions/qwen25-coder-3b.json`
- Reranking report: `outputs/qwen-evalplus-full-10-temp02/qwen3b-prediction-reranking-399.json`
- Candidate observations: `outputs/qwen-evalplus-full-10-temp02/output-clusters-v3/observations`
- Ranking code: `experiments/llm_prediction_reranker.py`

| K | MBPP | MBPP+ | Combined |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 1 | 60.15% | 49.62% | 48.87% |
| 2 | 63.41% | 52.38% | 51.63% |
| 3 | 64.66% | 54.64% | 53.63% |
| 4 | 65.16% | 55.39% | 54.39% |
| 5 | 65.66% | 55.64% | 54.64% |
| 6 | 65.66% | 55.64% | 54.64% |
| 7 | 65.91% | 55.89% | 54.89% |
| 8 | 66.17% | 56.64% | 55.64% |
| 9 | 66.42% | 56.89% | 55.89% |
| 10 | 66.42% | 56.89% | 55.89% |

Original-order baseline for the same candidates is 51.38%, 43.61%, and 42.86% at Pass@1 for MBPP, MBPP+, and combined, respectively. The Qwen 3B reranker therefore gains +8.77, +6.02, and +6.02 percentage points at Pass@1. Compared with the GPT-5 mini reranker, Qwen 3B is lower by 3.76 points on MBPP, 1.00 point on MBPP+, and 1.75 points combined at Pass@1. Both rerankers reach the same Pass@10 values because the candidate pool is unchanged.

Limitations: This evaluates the existing 10-candidate pool only. It measures selection quality after answer prediction, not code-generation quality. Candidate selection is independent of ground truth, while all reported metrics use ground truth after ranking.

## Qwen 7B standard-test answer prediction

Experiment: `qwen25-coder-7b-standard-answer-prediction`.

Setup: Qwen2.5-Coder-7B-Instruct was run locally with deterministic decoding on all 399 EvalPlus MBPP tasks and their 1,236 stored standard tests. The prompt asked the model to predict test outputs, using the same task and test format as the smaller-model answer-prediction experiment. Batch size was 2 and the generation limit was 256 new tokens. No candidate correctness labels were provided to the model.

Results: 919/1,236 standard-test outputs were correct, for 74.35% per-test accuracy. 212/399 tasks had every standard-test prediction correct, for 53.13% whole-task accuracy. Four responses were not parseable because they were truncated or malformed: Mbpp/462, Mbpp/255, Mbpp/108, and Mbpp/67. These are counted as incorrect, while the raw responses remain in the artifact.

Artifact: `outputs/qwen-answer-predictions/qwen25-coder-7b.json`.

## Qwen 7B answer-prediction cohort accuracy

Using the same ground-truth-defined candidate cohorts as the earlier Qwen 3B analysis, the mixed cohort contains 125 tasks where the ten saved generations include both correct and incorrect candidates. Qwen 7B predicted 291/385 standard outputs correctly, for 75.58% per-test accuracy. It predicted every standard output correctly on 67/125 tasks, for 53.60% whole-task accuracy. Cohort membership was determined from saved candidate labels before evaluating the Qwen predictions.

Full cohort report: `outputs/qwen-answer-predictions/qwen25-coder-7b-cohorts.json`.

Ties were broken by original candidate index. Ground-truth labels were used only after ranking to compute Pass@K. Four Qwen prediction rows had parse failures and therefore produced no predicted test values; their candidate rankings fall back to the deterministic original-index tie-break.

## Qwen 7B mean log-likelihood reranking on EvalPlus MBPP

Experiment: `qwen7b-loglikelihood-reranking-399`.

Qwen2.5-Coder-7B-Instruct scored each returned candidate answer with mean token log probability conditioned on the task prompt, entry point, one standard test, and an `ANSWER:` prefix. The run covered 11,907 candidate/test score slots, including 10,938 returned answers and 969 missing outputs. Missing outputs receive the lowest possible ranking treatment. Candidate selection used no correctness labels. The 10 candidates were ranked using seven aggregation rules, then ground-truth labels were joined to compute Pass@K.

Aggregation definitions:

- `mean`: arithmetic mean across available test scores.
- `median`: median across available test scores.
- `minimum`: lowest test score.
- `trimmed_mean`: removes the lowest and highest score when at least three scores are available, then averages the rest.
- `weighted_mean`: weights test positions 1, 2, and 3 as 1, 2, and 3. This is an exploratory order-weighted baseline.
- `rank_sum`: sums each candidate’s per-test rank, with lower rank sum preferred.
- `worst_penalty`: mean minus half the gap between the mean and the worst test score.

| Strategy | K | MBPP | MBPP+ |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| mean | 1 | 57.89% | 49.62% |
| mean | 2 | 60.40% | 52.38% |
| mean | 3 | 63.16% | 54.64% |
| mean | 4 | 63.91% | 54.89% |
| mean | 5 | 64.66% | 55.39% |
| mean | 6 | 65.16% | 56.14% |
| mean | 7 | 65.41% | 56.14% |
| mean | 8 | 65.91% | 56.64% |
| mean | 9 | 66.42% | 56.89% |
| mean | 10 | 66.42% | 56.89% |
| median | 1 | 58.15% | 49.87% |
| median | 2 | 60.40% | 51.63% |
| median | 3 | 61.90% | 52.63% |
| median | 4 | 62.91% | 53.88% |
| median | 5 | 63.16% | 54.14% |
| median | 6 | 64.41% | 55.39% |
| median | 7 | 64.91% | 55.64% |
| median | 8 | 65.91% | 56.64% |
| median | 9 | 66.42% | 56.89% |
| median | 10 | 66.42% | 56.89% |
| minimum | 1 | 56.39% | 48.37% |
| minimum | 2 | 59.40% | 50.88% |
| minimum | 3 | 61.40% | 52.88% |
| minimum | 4 | 61.90% | 53.38% |
| minimum | 5 | 62.91% | 54.14% |
| minimum | 6 | 63.91% | 54.89% |
| minimum | 7 | 64.66% | 55.64% |
| minimum | 8 | 65.66% | 56.14% |
| minimum | 9 | 66.17% | 56.39% |
| minimum | 10 | 66.42% | 56.89% |
| trimmed_mean | 1 | 52.38% | 44.86% |
| trimmed_mean | 2 | 56.14% | 48.12% |
| trimmed_mean | 3 | 58.40% | 50.13% |
| trimmed_mean | 4 | 59.90% | 51.63% |
| trimmed_mean | 5 | 61.40% | 52.88% |
| trimmed_mean | 6 | 62.66% | 53.88% |
| trimmed_mean | 7 | 63.91% | 54.89% |
| trimmed_mean | 8 | 64.91% | 56.14% |
| trimmed_mean | 9 | 66.17% | 56.64% |
| trimmed_mean | 10 | 66.42% | 56.89% |
| weighted_mean | 1 | 55.89% | 48.37% |
| weighted_mean | 2 | 58.65% | 51.13% |
| weighted_mean | 3 | 61.40% | 53.38% |
| weighted_mean | 4 | 61.90% | 53.63% |
| weighted_mean | 5 | 62.91% | 54.14% |
| weighted_mean | 6 | 63.66% | 54.89% |
| weighted_mean | 7 | 64.16% | 55.39% |
| weighted_mean | 8 | 65.16% | 56.39% |
| weighted_mean | 9 | 66.17% | 56.64% |
| weighted_mean | 10 | 66.42% | 56.89% |
| rank_sum | 1 | 56.89% | 47.37% |
| rank_sum | 2 | 60.65% | 50.88% |
| rank_sum | 3 | 63.16% | 53.63% |
| rank_sum | 4 | 63.91% | 54.14% |
| rank_sum | 5 | 64.41% | 54.89% |
| rank_sum | 6 | 65.41% | 55.89% |
| rank_sum | 7 | 65.91% | 56.39% |

| worst_penalty | 4 | 62.66% | 54.14% |
| worst_penalty | 5 | 63.91% | 54.64% |
| worst_penalty | 6 | 64.41% | 55.64% |
| worst_penalty | 7 | 65.16% | 55.89% |
| worst_penalty | 8 | 65.66% | 56.39% |
| worst_penalty | 9 | 66.17% | 56.64% |
| worst_penalty | 10 | 66.42% | 56.89% |

Original-order baseline for the same candidate pool is 51.38% MBPP and 43.61% MBPP+ at Pass@1. The best likelihood aggregation at Pass@1 is median on MBPP at 58.15% and median on MBPP+ at 49.87%.

## Visible prompt-assertion candidate filter

Experiment: `visible-assertion-filter-evalplus-399`.

Purpose: Measure how many of the ten saved candidate generations survive execution on the single assertion visibly embedded in each task's original MBPP prompt, before computing any benchmark Pass@K metrics.

Setup: Extracted the one `assert` statement from each raw task prompt and executed each of the ten saved candidate programs against that assertion. The 399 tasks were evaluated in 32 parallel worker processes with a two-second per-candidate timeout. The filter used no hidden standard tests, EvalPlus tests, canonical solutions, or correctness labels. Every candidate verdict and exception is retained.

| Surviving candidates | Tasks | Percentage |
|---:|---:|---:|
| 0 | 112 | 28.07% |
| 1 | 17 | 4.26% |
| 2 | 14 | 3.51% |
| 3 | 15 | 3.76% |
| 4 | 16 | 4.01% |
| 5 | 11 | 2.76% |
| 6 | 11 | 2.76% |
| 7 | 10 | 2.51% |
| 8 | 15 | 3.76% |
| 9 | 23 | 5.76% |
| 10 | 155 | 38.85% |

Artifact: `outputs/qwen-evalplus-full-10-temp02/visible-assertion-filter-399.json`.

No Pass@K metrics were computed in this experiment, as requested. The survivor set is saved for a separate follow-up evaluation.


## Pass@K after visible prompt-assertion filtering

The saved survivor lists were kept in original candidate order. Pass@K is computed over all 399 tasks, with the 112 tasks having no surviving candidate counted as misses. Conditional values over the 287 tasks with at least one survivor are also retained in the artifact.

| 3 | 63.41% | 53.63% | 53.13% | 88.15% | 74.56% | 73.87% |
| 4 | 63.66% | 53.88% | 53.38% | 88.50% | 74.91% | 74.22% |
| 5 | 63.91% | 54.14% | 53.63% | 88.85% | 75.26% | 74.56% |

Selecting the first surviving candidate is Pass@1. On the full suite, it achieves 62.41% MBPP, 50.63% MBPP+, and 50.13% combined. Conditional on a nonempty survivor set, these are 86.76%, 70.38%, and 69.69%.

The first surviving candidate is always the lowest-index candidate that passes the visible assertion. Ground-truth labels were used only after this selection for measurement.

Artifact: `outputs/qwen-evalplus-full-10-temp02/visible-assertion-filter-metrics-399.json`.


## Likelihood tiebreaker after visible-assertion filtering

After filtering by the one visible prompt assertion, surviving candidates were ranked using Qwen 7B likelihoods on the other two of the first three stored standard tests. Mean and median likelihood were evaluated separately. The filter left 287 tasks with survivors and 112 empty tasks. Five tasks did not have the visible example input among the first three stored standard tests; for those tasks, all three stored tests were used as a fallback. Empty survivor sets count as misses in the full-suite curves.

| K | Mean MBPP | Mean MBPP+ | Median MBPP | Median MBPP+ |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 1 | 61.90% | 51.13% | 62.16% | 51.13% |
| 2 | 62.91% | 52.88% | 63.16% | 52.88% |
| 3 | 63.41% | 53.88% | 63.66% | 53.88% |
| 4 | 63.41% | 53.88% | 63.66% | 53.88% |
| 5 | 63.66% | 53.88% | 63.66% | 53.88% |
| 6 | 63.66% | 54.39% | 63.66% | 54.39% |
| 7 | 63.91% | 54.39% | 63.91% | 54.39% |
| 8 | 63.91% | 54.64% | 63.91% | 54.64% |
| 9 | 64.16% | 54.89% | 64.16% | 54.89% |
| 10 | 64.16% | 54.89% | 64.16% | 54.89% |

Pass@1 is the selected first-ranked survivor. Mean likelihood gives 61.90% MBPP and 51.13% MBPP+. Median likelihood gives 62.16% MBPP and 51.13% MBPP+. Conditional on the 287 tasks with survivors, the corresponding MBPP and MBPP+ Pass@1 values are 86.06% and 71.08% for mean, and 86.41% and 71.08% for median.

The two scores are identical for most tasks because the normal case has exactly two remaining test scores, for which mean and median are equivalent. They differ on fallback or incomplete-score cases.

Artifacts: `outputs/qwen-evalplus-full-10-temp02/visible-assertion-likelihood-tiebreak-399.json`, `outputs/qwen-evalplus-full-10-temp02/visible-assertion-filter-399.json`, and `outputs/qwen-evalplus-full-10-temp02/qwen7b-loglikelihood-reranking-399.json`.

## Regenerated Qwen 3B visible-assertion filtering

The original 1.5B and 3B candidate pools were unrecoverable, so the Qwen2.5-Coder-3B-Instruct pool was regenerated and archived before evaluation. Generation used the original Qwen ChatML prompt, ten samples per task, temperature 0.2, top_p 0.95, and max_new_tokens 512. Raw completions, cleaned Python files, per-task manifests, and a run manifest are in `outputs/regenerated-qwen25-coder-3b-10-temp02` and the compressed archive `outputs/regenerated-qwen25-coder-3b-10-temp02-candidates.tar.zst`.

Each regenerated candidate was tested against the one assertion visible in its task prompt. The first surviving candidate in original order was then evaluated against canonical MBPP and MBPP+ inputs. Of 399 tasks, 341 had at least one survivor and 58 had none. Full-suite first-survivor accuracy was 299/399 = 74.94% on MBPP and 242/399 = 60.65% on MBPP+. Conditional accuracy among tasks with a survivor was 87.68% MBPP and 70.97% MBPP+.

The evaluation artifact is `outputs/regenerated-qwen25-coder-3b-10-temp02/visible-assertion-filter-evaluation.json`. These values come from a newly sampled candidate pool and should not be compared as an exact paired reranking result with the earlier deleted 3B run.

The 1.5B checkpoint was not available locally. Three Hugging Face download attempts returned HTTP 429, so no 1.5B candidates were regenerated. The attempts are recorded in `outputs/regenerated-qwen25-coder-1p5b-10-temp02/download_attempts.json`.

## Regenerated Qwen 3B visible-assertion survivor diagnosis

Experiment: diagnose whether visible-assertion filtering removed all correct candidates and whether first-survivor ranking selected an incorrect candidate when a later survivor was correct.

Purpose: Decompose errors among the regenerated Qwen2.5-Coder-3B-Instruct candidate pool after execution of the one assertion visible in each task prompt.

Setup: The pool contains 399 EvalPlus MBPP tasks with ten regenerated candidates per task. Filtering used only the visible prompt assertion and retained survivors in original candidate order. For this diagnostic, every one of the 3,014 survivors across the 341 nonempty tasks was executed on the canonical MBPP base inputs in a fresh subprocess. Input objects were transported with pickle so tuple and list types were preserved. Correctness labels were used only after the survivor set and first-survivor choice were fixed. The corrected analysis artifact is outputs/regenerated-qwen25-coder-3b-10-temp02/base-survivor-correctness-analysis.json, and the selection artifact is outputs/regenerated-qwen25-coder-3b-10-temp02/visible-assertion-filter-evaluation.json.

Results among the 341 tasks with at least one survivor:

- Filtering removed every correct candidate on 35 tasks, which is 10.26% of nonempty tasks. Therefore at least one correct candidate survived on 306/341 tasks, or 89.74%.
- There were 332 tasks with multiple survivors.
- Among the 299 multi-survivor tasks with at least one correct survivor, the first survivor was incorrect on 7 tasks. This is an incorrect tie-break rate of 2.34% among multi-survivor tasks with a correct option.
- Counting all 332 multi-survivor tasks as the denominator, the first-survivor tie-break error rate was 2.11%.
- The first survivor was correct on 299/341 nonempty tasks, or 87.68% conditional accuracy. The same result is 299/399, or 74.94%, over the full 399-task suite because 58 empty survivor sets count as misses.

The seven incorrect first-survivor selections were Mbpp/92, Mbpp/119, Mbpp/123, Mbpp/223, Mbpp/643, Mbpp/773, and Mbpp/780. The 35 filter false negatives were recorded in the JSON artifact. The corrected run agrees with the original selected-candidate evaluation on all 341 nonempty tasks.

Limitations: These are MBPP base-suite correctness diagnostics for the newly regenerated 3B candidate pool. They do not use MBPP+ labels and are not an exact paired comparison with the deleted earlier 3B candidate pool.

## Correction: true assertion-filter false negatives for regenerated Qwen 3B

The earlier 35-task count was incorrectly described as cases where the filter removed a correct candidate. That analysis evaluated only survivors, so 35 meant that no correct survivor existed. The expanded run evaluated all 3,990 regenerated candidates on canonical MBPP base inputs.

Among the 399 tasks, 308 had at least one correct candidate before filtering. The visible assertion filter removed all correct candidates on only 2 tasks, Mbpp/400 and Mbpp/442, giving a true filter false-negative rate of 2/308 = 0.65% among tasks with any correct candidate, or 2/341 = 0.59% among tasks with at least one survivor. The remaining 33 of the earlier 35 cases had no correct candidate among all ten generations. The expanded per-candidate report is outputs/regenerated-qwen25-coder-3b-10-temp02/base-survivor-correctness-analysis.json.

Mbpp/400 has a visible assertion claiming four distinct tuples have three unique tuples, while the correct function returns four. Mbpp/442 compares the exact positive ratio 7/13 against the rounded literal 0.54, which fails exact equality even though the generated function is correct on canonical tests.

The retained-correct count remains 306 tasks. Of these, 7 were lost by the first-survivor tie break, leaving 299 currently selected correctly.

## Candidate-level confusion matrix for regenerated Qwen 3B assertion filter

The confusion matrix treats each of the 3,990 regenerated candidate programs as one example. The positive label is canonical MBPP base correctness, and the positive filter decision is survival after executing the one visible prompt assertion. Ground-truth correctness is used only to measure the filter after selection.

- True positives: 2,675 correct generations kept by the filter.
- True negatives: 970 incorrect generations rejected by the filter.
- False positives: 339 incorrect generations kept by the filter.
- False negatives: 6 correct generations rejected by the filter.

The 3,014 survivors equal 2,675 true positives plus 339 false positives. The 976 rejected candidates equal 970 true negatives plus 6 false negatives. The six false-negative candidates occur on the two tasks Mbpp/400 and Mbpp/442.

## Adaptive assertion-guided Qwen 3B pilot on Mbpp/2

Experiment: `adaptive-assertion-guided-qwen3b-mbpp-2`.

Setup: Qwen2.5-Coder-3B-Instruct used the original ChatML task prompt with temperature 0.2, top_p 0.95, and max_new_tokens 512. The loop allowed up to ten total model outputs, repaired a failed candidate using its visible assertion error, and stopped immediately at the first passing candidate. The candidate passed the visible assertion on the first generation, so no repair call was needed.

Artifact: `outputs/adaptive-qwen25-coder-3b-pilot/mbpp_2.json`. The artifact saves the complete prompt, raw response, cleaned code, verdict, task, assertion, model, and stop-on-first-pass configuration.


------------------------------------------------------------------------
CASCADE EVALUATION RESULTS
Experiment: qwen3b-simple-cascade
Date: 2026-09-13
Purpose: Measure a 0.5B initial generation with one 3B fallback on visible assertion failures.
Setup: Qwen2.5-Coder-0.5B-Instruct generated all 399 EvalPlus MBPP tasks. Visible assertion failures were routed to Qwen2.5-Coder-3B-Instruct for one repair. Candidates were saved immediately per task. Final candidates were evaluated against canonical MBPP base and MBPP+ inputs after routing.
Configuration: temperature=0.2, top_p=0.95, max_new_tokens=512, one total retry, 199 of 399 tasks routed to 3B.
Selection procedure: The visible assertion alone selected whether to route. MBPP and MBPP+ correctness labels were used only for the separate benchmark measurement.
Artifacts: outputs/qwen25-coder-cascade-full; outputs/qwen25-coder-cascade-full/metrics.json; logs/cascade_run.log
Code commit: 43617b9
Metrics denominator: 397 evaluable tasks. Two tasks had canonical reference timeouts and were recorded separately.
Initial 0.5B MBPP: 180/397 = 0.4534005038
Final cascade MBPP: 222/397 = 0.5591939547
Initial 0.5B MBPP+: 148/397 = 0.3727959698
Final cascade MBPP+: 184/397 = 0.4634760705
Paired gain: +42 MBPP tasks and +36 MBPP+ tasks.
Environment: Python local workspace /workspace/post-training, Qwen models loaded with Transformers and bfloat16, process based benchmark evaluation with ten second canonical reference timeout.
Limitations: This is a stochastic single run. The two timed out canonical references are excluded from the reported denominator.
## Ten-generation baseline versus visible-assertion filtering

Experiment: ten-generation-baseline-versus-visible-assertion-filter

Setup: The saved 0.5B and 3B pools each contain ten candidates per EvalPlus MBPP task. Baseline Pass@K uses the first K candidates in generation order. Filtering selects the first candidate that passes the prompt-visible assertion. Hidden MBPP and MBPP+ labels are used only after this selection. Two canonical reference tasks timed out, so all metrics below use 397 common evaluable tasks.

## Official-protocol greedy Qwen EvalPlus rerun

Experiment: official-protocol-greedy-qwen-evalplus-2026-09-14

Purpose: Reevaluate Qwen2.5-Coder-Instruct 0.5B, 1.5B, and 3B after correcting the protocol mismatch with the published Qwen evaluation.

Generation setup: One completion per each of the 399 EvalPlus MBPP tasks. The runner used the published ChatML prompt with task whitespace stripped, greedy decoding, temperature 0, max_new_tokens 2048, and the Qwen stop strings. Generation used the local Transformers backend because the repository did not contain the original vLLM environment. Official EvalPlus 0.2.1 scoring was applied to the generated task directories.

| Model | MBPP Pass@1 | MBPP+ Pass@1 |
|---|---:|---:|
| Qwen2.5-Coder-0.5B-Instruct | 51.6% | 43.4% |
| Qwen2.5-Coder-1.5B-Instruct | 63.4% | 53.9% |
| Qwen2.5-Coder-3B-Instruct | 71.2% | 60.4% |

Artifacts: outputs/qwen-official-greedy-0.5B, outputs/qwen-official-greedy-1.5B, and outputs/qwen-official-greedy-3B. Generation code commit: 48441d6.

Comparison: The Qwen report lists 52.4% MBPP and 43.7% MBPP+ for 0.5B-Instruct, 69.2% and 59.4% for 1.5B-Instruct, and 73.6% and 62.4% for 3B-Instruct. The rerun closes most of the earlier 3B discrepancy, from 67.25% / 55.16% to 71.2% / 60.4%. The remaining differences may reflect the Transformers versus vLLM backend and exact checkpoint or environment details.

EXPERIMENT: Canonical EvalPlus task separation and prior GRPO checkpoint audit, 2026-09-19.
PURPOSE: Diagnose falling training reward and nearly flat greedy MBPP+ performance, then verify a disjoint training split.
SETUP: Qwen/Qwen2.5-Coder-3B-Instruct with LoRA rank 16, alpha 32, dropout 0.05. GRPO uses four temperature-sampled completions per training prompt, 2048 completion tokens, original MBPP assertion pass fraction as reward, batch size one, accumulation 32, and seed 42. The prior W&B run divine-disco-69 (buaj3il3) used learning rate 1e-6. The interrupted replacement run celestial-wildflower-70 (nka4qb9h) used 3e-6 and had no usable step history.
DATA: Hugging Face google-research-datasets/mbpp train, validation, and test contain 964 tasks. Hugging Face evalplus/mbppplus has 378 tasks. The installed evalplus.data.get_mbpp_plus() canonical runner has 399. The old 593-task training set overlapped 21 canonical evaluation tasks. The corrected training set contains 572 tasks with zero canonical overlap. Canonical EvalPlus evaluates all 399 tasks using greedy generation and both base and augmented tests.
SELECTION: Best checkpoint was selected by canonical MBPP+ pass@1 after each ten optimizer steps. The analysis compares step 0 and saved step 40; these scores are benchmark measurements, and the overlap in the old data invalidates interpreting them as fully held out.
METRICS: Prior W&B rolling reward was approximately 0.671 at step 20, 0.489 at step 39, and 0.534 at step 47. Canonical step-zero base pass was 294/399 and MBPP+ pass was 245/399. Step-40 base pass was 293/399 and MBPP+ pass was 249/399. Among the 21 leaked tasks, MBPP+ pass was 10/21 at both steps; among the other 378, it was 235/378 at step 0 and 239/378 at step 40. The clean partition regression test passed, and actual dataset preparation returned 572 training records with zero canonical overlap.
ARTIFACTS: outputs/grpo-mbpp/evalplus/step-0 and step-40 contain prior result JSON. The corrected run uses outputs/grpo-mbpp-canonical-split-lr3e6. Code is the commit named Correct canonical EvalPlus task exclusion and isolate the 3e-6 GRPO run.
LIMITATIONS: The old training run had benchmark contamination. Rolling rewards use changing task batches and cannot by themselves establish policy degradation. The small four-task MBPP+ gain is within a low-resolution 399-task comparison, and the 3e-6 replacement had not produced outcomes at the time of this record.

## 2026-09-20: Retrospective for killed hybrid GRPO run

Experiment: `cool-fog-74` (`dizijcbf`). Purpose: determine whether hybrid GRPO improved the Qwen2.5-Coder-3B model on the clean 572-task MBPP training split and the separate 399-task canonical EvalPlus MBPP evaluation split.

Setup: LoRA GRPO with `learning_rate: 3e-6`, `num_train_epochs: 10`, `per_device_train_batch_size: 1`, `num_generations: 4`, hybrid reward coefficient 0.5, vLLM evaluation, greedy 2,048-token evaluation generations, and evaluation every 10 optimizer steps. Training used original MBPP tests. Evaluation used canonical MBPP and MBPP+ tests. W&B run: `kunal-personal/grpo-mbpp/dizijcbf`. Output directory: `outputs/grpo-mbpp-canonical-split-hybrid-lr3e6`.

Evaluation metrics at step 0 and the final recorded evaluation step 520 were: training pass@1 `0.6294` to `0.6329`, training test fraction `0.6871` to `0.6935`, evaluation MBPP pass@1 `0.737` to `0.732`, evaluation MBPP test fraction `0.7791` to `0.7743`, evaluation MBPP+ pass@1 `0.614` to `0.619`, and evaluation MBPP+ test fraction `0.7700` to `0.7703`.

Training batch metrics were noisy. The final W&B summary reported reward `0.6510`, test progress `0.6771`, full-pass fraction `0.6250`, all-zero fraction `0.2813`, rolling reward `0.5813`, loss `0.0314`, gradient norm `0.0387`, parameter update norm `0.001288`, and relative parameter update `0.0000351`.

Conclusion: this run did not demonstrate a reliable benchmark improvement. MBPP+ pass@1 rose by two tasks from 245/399 to 247/399 at the final checkpoint, while MBPP pass@1 fell by two tasks. The changes are within the expected discrete sampling noise. The run was killed after evaluation step 520. Candidate selection was not performed from the evaluation metrics.

Limitations: the run summary omitted the reward configuration fields, although the launched output directory and prior configuration identify this as the hybrid run. The final recorded checkpoint was not saved as a selected best checkpoint because no benchmark improvement was established.

## 2026-09-20: Step zero versus final generation comparison

Compared the saved step 0 and step 520 generations for W&B run `cool-fog-74` (`dizijcbf`) task by task. Training changed 22 of 572 tasks: 7 newly full passing, 5 full-pass regressions, 7 partial improvements, and 3 partial regressions. Eval MBPP changed 12 of 399 tasks: 5 newly passing and 7 regressions. Eval MBPP+ changed 8 of 399 tasks: 5 newly passing and 3 regressions. The complete per-task records and both saved completions are in `outputs/grpo-mbpp-canonical-split-hybrid-lr3e6/step-0-vs-520-comparison.json`.

The task-level evidence shows real semantic changes, including a corrected bitonic subsequence sum and a print-to-return fix, alongside regressions involving tuple versus list output and an off-by-one slice. The result supports task churn rather than broad improvement. The comparison used the full training and evaluation denominators and kept candidate selection independent of the reported evaluation measurements.

## 2026-09-20: Repeated sampled completions for base versus step 50 adapter

Experiment: `repeated-sampling-base-vs-step50`. Purpose: test whether the trained adapter improves sampled solution quality on fixed training prompts even when greedy checkpoint evaluation is flat.

Setup: selected 32 of the 572 disjoint MBPP training tasks with seed 42. Generated 16 completions per task for both `Qwen/Qwen2.5-Coder-3B-Instruct` and `outputs/grpo-mbpp-canonical-split-hybrid-lr3e6/best_checkpoints/step-50`. Both models used the same canonical training prompts, temperature 1.0, top-p 1.0, 2,048 maximum tokens, and seed 42. Each completion was scored against the original MBPP tests with the hybrid reward coefficient 0.5. Candidate selection was independent of evaluation ground truth.

Results over 512 completions per model: mean hybrid reward was `0.519857` for the base and `0.523438` for the adapter, mean test fraction was `0.545573` and `0.548828`, full-pass fraction was `0.494141` and `0.498047`, and zero-test fraction was `0.402344` and `0.392578`. The adapter change was therefore +0.003581 reward, +0.003255 test fraction, +0.003906 full-pass fraction, and -0.009766 zero-test fraction.

Diversity results over 32 task groups of 16 samples were identical for both models: flat reward group fraction `0.15625`, flat test-fraction group fraction `0.15625`, and mean unique completion fraction `0.992188`. The full-pass count histogram changed, but the aggregate difference was two completions and is within the sampling uncertainty of 512 completions.

Conclusion: the adapter did not show a meaningful improvement in sampled behavior on this fixed subset. It also did not reduce completion diversity. The results support the earlier conclusion that the trained policy is only slightly different from the base model.

Artifact: `outputs/grpo-mbpp-canonical-split-hybrid-lr3e6/repeated-sampling-step50-vs-base.json`.

Artifacts: outputs/fixed-rollout-overfit/result-adamw-lr1e-5-beta1e-3.json, result-adamw-lr1e-5-beta3e-3.json, result-adamw-lr1e-5-beta1e-2.json, result-adamw-lr1e-5-beta3e-2.json, and result-adamw-lr1e-5-beta1e-1.json. Results: beta0.001 loss-0.170898 to-1.534928, positive delta+0.228172, negative delta-6.261951; beta0.003 loss to-1.265929, positive+0.228017, negative-5.092626; beta0.01 loss to-0.980362, positive+0.228017, negative-3.948444; beta0.03 loss to-0.738488, positive+0.228094, negative-2.889151; beta0.1 loss to-0.514180, positive+0.228094, negative-1.771031. Candidate selection is independent of ground truth. The sweep identifies beta0.01 to0.03 as middle candidates, but a two-completion probe cannot select the final training value.

FIXED ROLLOUT LEARNING RATE SWEEP, 2026-09-21. Beta was fixed at0.01 and the same frozen two-completion setup used constant AdamW for100 updates. Artifacts: outputs/fixed-rollout-overfit/result-beta1e-2-lr1e-6.json, result-beta1e-2-lr3e-6.json, result-adamw-lr1e-5-beta1e-2.json, result-beta1e-2-lr3e-5.json, and result-beta1e-2-lr1e-4.json. Results: lr1e-6 loss delta-0.05441, positive delta-0.0122, negative-0.2304; lr3e-6 loss delta-0.69820, positive-0.0199, negative-3.2372; lr1e-5 loss delta-0.80946, positive+0.2280, negative-3.9484; lr3e-5 loss delta-0.80986, positive+0.2296, negative-3.9171; lr1e-4 loss delta-0.80991, positive+0.2297, negative-3.9406. Candidate selection is independent of ground truth. Rates at or above1e-5 converge to nearly the same KL-constrained two-completion behavior, so this probe favors1e-5 as the practical rate.

FIXED ROLLOUT REWARD COEFFICIENT SWEEP, 2026-09-21. Beta was fixed at0.01 and constant AdamW learning rate1e-5 for100 updates. The four fixed candidates had test fractions1.0,0.5,0.2,0.0 and full-pass indicators1,0,0,0. Artifacts: outputs/fixed-rollout-overfit/result-rewardcoef0.json, result-rewardcoef025.json, result-rewardcoef05.json, result-rewardcoef075.json, and result-rewardcoef1.json. Results for coefficients0,0.25,0.5,0.75,1.0: full completion log-probability delta+0.817 for each; high-partial delta-0.265,-1.754,-3.024,-3.562,-3.769; low-partial delta-3.464,-4.112,-4.193,-4.161,-4.069; zero-reward delta-4.569,-4.448,-4.303,-4.148,-3.874. Candidate selection is independent of ground truth. The sweep shows the coefficient controls whether partial solutions remain competitive with full solutions.

## Experiment: tiny fixed-set GRPO overfit diagnostic, 16 generations

Status: running as of 2026-09-20.

Purpose: test whether GRPO can improve greedy pass@1 on the exact small set used for training before investigating held-out generalization.

Setup: Qwen/Qwen2.5-Coder-3B-Instruct with LoRA GRPO, hybrid reward `0.5 * all_tests_passed + 0.5 * original_MBPP_test_fraction`, eight records selected by `max_train_samples: 8` after excluding the canonical EvalPlus MBPP task IDs, 16 generations per prompt, generation batch size 64, 32 gradient accumulation steps, learning rate `3e-6`, maximum 50 optimizer steps, 2048 maximum completion tokens, bfloat16, greedy vLLM evaluation, and evaluation every five optimizer steps. Canonical 399 task EvalPlus evaluation is disabled. Step zero, intermediate checkpoints, and the final model use the same eight training records and original MBPP tests. Candidate selection uses only this tiny-set pass@1. No held-out ground truth is used for selection.

Artifacts: W&B project `kunal-personal/grpo-mbpp`, run `grpo-mbpp-tiny-overfit-g16`, ID `kvv1cohr`; output directory `outputs/grpo-mbpp-tiny-overfit-g16`; code commit `80d70db`.

Preliminary step-zero metrics: pass@1 `0.625` (5 of 8 tasks), overall original MBPP test pass fraction `0.75`. Final and intermediate metrics are pending.

Limitations: this is an intentional overfit diagnostic on eight training tasks and cannot estimate held-out EvalPlus performance.

DETERMINISTIC TWO-COMPLETION GRPO PROBE
Experiment: deterministic-grpo-probe-high-rate
Purpose: Isolate one fixed policy-gradient update from rollout sampling, task scoring, batching, and scheduler effects.
Timestamp: 2026-09-20 UTC
Git commit: d8020a8
Candidate artifact: outputs/deterministic-grpo-probe/result.json
Selection procedure: No candidate selection was performed.
Configuration: Qwen/Qwen2.5-Coder-3B-Instruct, rank-16 LoRA targeting q_proj, k_proj, v_proj, o_proj, gate_proj, up_proj, and down_proj, dropout disabled through evaluation mode, one fixed prompt, fixed completions, advantages [+0.5, -0.5], one SGD update, learning rate 1e-3.
Metrics:
  - Before log probabilities: [-0.229757, -0.913350].
  - After log probabilities: [-0.236541, -0.917835].
  - Log-probability deltas: [-0.006785, -0.004485].
  - Positive direction passed: false. Negative direction passed: true.
  - Policy loss changed from -0.170898 to -0.170324, which is an increase rather than a decrease.
  - Gradient norm: 0.252416.
Interpretation: At this deliberately large diagnostic learning rate, the positive completion did not become more likely. Since the rate is 333 times larger than the training rate, this result may reflect an overly large nonlinear step or shared-parameter interference. It requires a matched 3e-6 rerun before diagnosing the loss sign.

DETERMINISTIC TWO-COMPLETION GRPO PROBE AT TRAINING RATE
Experiment: deterministic-grpo-probe-3e-6
Purpose: Repeat the deterministic two-completion test at the actual GRPO learning rate.
Timestamp: 2026-09-20 UTC
Git commit: d53b562
Candidate artifact: outputs/deterministic-grpo-probe/result-3e-6.json
Configuration: Same fixed prompt, completions, advantages, rank-16 LoRA, evaluation mode, and one SGD update as the high-rate probe, with learning rate 3e-6.
Metrics:
  - Before log probabilities: [-0.229757, -0.913350].
  - After log probabilities: [-0.230913, -0.915099].
  - Log-probability deltas: [-0.001157, -0.001749].
  - Positive completion direction passed: false. Negative completion direction passed: true.
  - Policy loss changed from -0.170898 to -0.171047, which is a decrease.
  - Gradient norm: 0.252416.
Interpretation: The aggregate policy objective moved in the correct direction at the real training rate. The positive completion nevertheless became slightly less likely individually because the shared LoRA update reduced the negative completion likelihood more strongly. This rules out a simple global loss-sign failure and shows that individual sample likelihood movement is not a sufficient GRPO correctness test.

GRPO reward audit, 2026-09-20, code 6dc5e1f. Complete setup, control results, versions, trace digest and reproduction scripts are preserved in logs/grpo_reward_audit.md. All 128 v4 rewards replayed identically. 78/128 raw completions had over 30 discarded characters. Retokenized raw/prefix totals were 23628/12805. Zero-rate control yielded zero changes. Float32 SGD at 1e-3 decreased loss by approximately 6.26e-5, unlike the prior bfloat16 result. Exact raw controls are embedded in the report. These are diagnostics, not benchmark results. No candidates were selected using ground truth.

INTERRUPTED MASKED TINY OVERFIT ATTEMPT
Experiment: grpo-mbpp-tiny-overfit-g16-reward-masked
Purpose: First attempt to test reward-prefix loss masking on eight fixed training tasks.
Timestamp: 2026-09-21 UTC
Git commit: c6f1544
W&B run: i7k4wij5
Data and setup: Eight disjoint MBPP training tasks, rank-16 LoRA, hybrid reward, DAPO, 3e-6 learning rate, 16 completions per group, and greedy evaluation every five updates.
Outcome: Stopped after step 10 when audit found that the new token mask did not update DAPO's raw-token denominator. Step-zero and step-five greedy pass@1 were both 0.625 and test-pass fraction was 0.75. These values are not used to assess the repaired implementation.
Limitation: The masked numerator with an unmasked denominator diluted the objective. A new output directory and run will use the masked denominator and likelihood probe.

INTERIM STOP AND LIKELIHOOD AUDIT, 2026-09-21. Run urzbevgw, source6903599. Complete setup, artifact identifiers, selection policy, metrics and limitations are recorded in the accompanying ERROR ANALYSIS entry. Step0 to5:5/8 full passes unchanged;18/24 to20/24 tests. Contextual stop mismatch reproduced in3/5 targeted CPU examples. No new training or benchmark execution.

CONTEXTUAL STOP AND PROBE SPEED REPAIR, 2026-09-21. Code source is the relaunch commit. The prior run urzbevgw was stopped after six updates. The new run will use Qwen/Qwen2.5-Coder-3B-Instruct, seed42, eight fixed disjoint MBPP training tasks, 16 sampled completions per group, hybrid reward coefficient0.5, original MBPP tests, LoRA rank16 alpha32 dropout0.05, DAPO beta0, learning rate3e-6 with linear decay, bfloat16, max completion2048, gradient accumulation32, probe batch size4, and the configured step5 greedy same-task evaluation. Candidate selection is by fixed greedy evaluation procedure; ground-truth tests are used afterward only for measurement. Artifact output is outputs/grpo-mbpp-tiny-overfit-g16-reward-masked-dapo-contextual; W&B run identifier will be recorded at launch. Fixes include tokenizer-offset reward masking, Transformers context-aware stopping, masked length reporting, and one batched probe likelihood pass. The previous baseline was step0 5/8 pass@1 and18/24 tests; step5 was5/8 and20/24. No result from the new run yet.

RELAUNCH INTERIM RESULT, 2026-09-21. W&B run cqaaddy7 is running from source commit dedf457 with the complete setup above. After two optimizer updates, step1 reward was0.375 and step2 had no new rollout reward yet because it reused the generation batch. Probe step1 had DAPO delta-0.000204, advantage correlation0.150, sign alignment0.5625; step2 had delta-0.000629, correlation0.032, sign alignment0.625. First update wall time83 seconds and second27 seconds. No final selection or benchmark result yet.

METRIC LOGGING UPDATE, 2026-09-21. Source change adds training rollout test_pass_fraction, partial_pass_fraction, and partial_or_full_pass_fraction, plus matching training evaluation fields. The active W&B run cqaaddy7 predates this code change, so no new numerical result is claimed from these fields yet. Validation: 27 focused tests passed.

CANONICAL PARTIAL METRIC UPDATE, 2026-09-21. Added MBPP and MBPP+ partial-only and partial-or-full completion rates to canonical EvalPlus metric summaries. No new experiment result claimed. Validation: 27 focused tests passed.

FIXED ROLLOUT OVERFIT DIAGNOSTIC, 2026-09-21. Purpose: test whether the policy optimizer can maximize a frozen reward and old-log-probability batch without new generation or sandbox noise. The original live run cqaaddy7 was stopped. Source commit was the fixed-probe update before its final AdamW patch; final code artifact is experiments/deterministic_grpo_probe.py and result output is outputs/fixed-rollout-overfit/result-adamw-lr3e-6.json. Setup: Qwen/Qwen2.5-Coder-3B-Instruct, seed42, one fixed ChatML prompt, correct add completion advantage+0.5, incorrect subtract completion advantage-0.5, LoRA rank16 alpha32 dropout0, AdamW, learning rate3e-6,100 repeated updates, deterministic evaluation mode. Candidate selection was fixed before measuring the objective; no ground-truth selection occurred. Result: loss-0.170898 to-1.012714;94/100 update deltas negative and6 positive; positive log-probability delta-0.060516; negative log-probability delta-3.427778. The frozen objective was strongly optimized by suppressing the negative completion. Limitation: two completions and one simple prompt do not reproduce the multi-task rollout geometry or fresh sampling. No benchmark result claimed.

FIXED ROLLOUT HIGH-RATE DIAGNOSTIC, 2026-09-21. Same fixed prompt, completions, advantages, model, LoRA, AdamW, and deterministic evaluation as the preceding diagnostic, with constant learning rate1e-4 and100 updates. Artifact: outputs/fixed-rollout-overfit/result-adamw-lr1e-4.json. Result: loss-0.170898 to-12.406243;95/100 updates improved and5 worsened; positive log-probability delta-2.464407; negative delta-51.405787. The objective was optimized aggressively by suppressing the negative completion. This is a mechanism result, not a benchmark result, and does not justify using1e-4 for full GRPO.

FIXED ROLLOUT INTERMEDIATE-RATE DIAGNOSTIC, 2026-09-21. Same fixed setup with constant AdamW learning rate1e-5 and100 updates. Artifact: outputs/fixed-rollout-overfit/result-adamw-lr1e-5.json. Result: loss-0.170898 to-6.337874;100/100 updates improved; positive log-probability delta-0.309648; negative delta-24.977552. The frozen objective is stable at this rate but still improves mainly by suppressing the negative completion. This is a mechanism result, not a benchmark result.

FIXED ROLLOUT KL CONTROL, 2026-09-21. Same fixed prompt and completions, constant AdamW1e-5,100 updates, beta0.01, no auxiliary term. Artifact: outputs/fixed-rollout-overfit/result-adamw-lr1e-5-beta1e-2.json. Loss-0.170898 to-0.980362;82 updates improved and18 worsened; positive log-probability delta+0.228017; negative delta-3.948444.

FIXED ROLLOUT KL PLUS POSITIVE LIKELIHOOD CONTROL, 2026-09-21. Same setup with beta0.01 and positive-likelihood coefficient0.05. Artifact: outputs/fixed-rollout-overfit/result-adamw-lr1e-5-beta1e-2-pos05.json. Loss-0.159411 to-0.980452;80 updates improved and20 worsened; positive delta+0.228562; negative delta-3.918873. The KL-only and KL-plus-auxiliary results were nearly identical.

EXPERIMENT: Single-task GRPO overfit on MBPP task 601

Purpose. This run tests whether the configured GRPO setup can increase sampled reward on one fixed task.

Code and setup. The run used repository commit 409a510 and configuration file configs/tiny_single_task_overfit.yaml. It used Qwen/Qwen2.5-Coder-3B-Instruct, seed 42, the filtered google-research-datasets/mbpp training data, and the first remaining training record, task 601. The evaluation used one greedy completion at step 0 and every five optimizer steps through step 50, scored against that task's original MBPP tests. The run used LoRA rank 16, alpha 32, dropout 0.05, DAPO loss, learning rate 1e-5 with the configured linear schedule, beta 0.01, hybrid reward coefficient 0.75, 16 generations, physical batch size 1, gradient accumulation 32, generation batch size 16, maximum prompt length 512, maximum completion length 2048, bf16, reward-stop masking, and a three-second sandbox timeout. Generation batch size 16 is the smallest complete group for one prompt with 16 generations. The original value 64 caused TRL to drop the incomplete four-prompt sampler batch for a one-row dataset.

Runtime and identifiers. The run used TRL 0.25.0, Transformers 4.56.2, Accelerate 1.15.0, vLLM 0.10.2, PyTorch 2.8.0+cu126, and one NVIDIA RTX 4000 Ada GPU with 20 GiB. It completed 50 optimizer steps in 2712.576 seconds. The W&B run is https://wandb.ai/kunal-personal/grpo-mbpp/runs/lvbc068l. Artifacts are in outputs/grpo-mbpp-single-task-g16-hybrid075-beta001-lr1e5-retry2, with per-checkpoint metrics and details and a final saved adapter directory.

Selection and benchmark boundary. Task 601 was selected by dataset order, without screening tasks by their test outcomes. The task was used for training reward and for the requested in-sample checkpoint evaluations. Its step-zero greedy completion already passed all tests. Every checkpoint from step 5 through step 50 tied at pass@1 1.0, so the configured pass@1 selector retained step 5 as the first best checkpoint. This diagnostic is not a held-out benchmark measurement, and checkpoint selection used the same task tests as training reward.

Results. Step-zero and all ten checkpoint evaluations had pass@1 1.0, test pass fraction 1.0, and average reward 1.0. The sampled reward mean averaged 0.4943 in steps 1 through 10, 0.5094 in steps 11 through 20, 0.5724 in steps 21 through 30, 0.5365 in steps 31 through 40, and 0.6359 in steps 41 through 50. Sampled full-pass fraction averaged 0.4875 in the first ten steps and 0.6312 in the last ten. Sampled test progress averaged 0.5146 and 0.6500 over those same windows. Individual rollout means remained volatile. The step-50 reward mean was 0.5156, and the overall average reward was 0.5497.

Limitations. Greedy pass rate had no headroom because the base model already solved task 601. The reward trend is based on stochastic rollouts of one task and does not establish generalization. The in-training final vLLM evaluation could not initialize because only 12.68 GiB was free while vLLM requested 17.6 GiB. After the training process exited, a standalone evaluation of the saved selected adapter succeeded with pass@1 1.0 and full test coverage. The final artifact is outputs/grpo-mbpp-single-task-g16-hybrid075-beta001-lr1e5-retry2/training-final-metrics.json.

------------------------------------------------------------------------
EXPERIMENT: Full-data MBPP GRPO with 0.5B model, 8 task groups x 16 generations

Status. Completed all 50 optimizer updates. The final adapter is in `outputs/grpo-mbpp-fulltrain-8x16-b128-micro2-workers8-0.5b-20260924/final`.

Purpose. Test fresh-rollout GRPO on the complete non-EvalPlus MBPP task pool while preserving 128 completions per optimizer update, parallelizing reward execution, and using a larger GPU microbatch.

Command. `HF_HUB_ENABLE_HF_TRANSFER=0 accelerate launch --num_processes 1 train.py --config configs/grpo-mbpp-fulltrain-8x16-b128-micro2-workers8-0.5b-20260924.yaml`.

Configuration and data. The committed YAML uses `Qwen/Qwen2.5-Coder-0.5B-Instruct`, seed 42, all 593 normalized MBPP rows remaining after EvalPlus task-ID exclusion, fresh rollouts, 8 task groups per update, 16 generations per task, generation batch 128, per-device training microbatch 2, gradient accumulation 64, DAPO loss, learning rate 1e-5, beta 0.01, LoRA rank 16 / alpha 32 / dropout 0.05, hybrid reward coefficient 0.75, maximum prompt/completion lengths 512/2048, bf16, three-second sandbox timeout, eight bounded reward-scoring workers, 50 optimizer steps, and evaluation every 10 steps. The reward trace confirms 6,400 total completions from exactly 400 distinct task IDs, with 16 completions per task. Thus 400 of the 593 eligible tasks were sampled once and 193 received no rollout during this 50-update run.

Evaluation and selection. The step-zero and scheduled evaluations report greedy pass@1 and test-pass fraction on the same 593-row training pool. They are in-sample, not validation or held-out benchmark results. `select_best_checkpoint: false` prevents these ground-truth metrics from choosing a checkpoint. The fixed step-50 policy is the selected artifact if the run completes. Candidate selection is therefore independent of evaluation ground truth; reward tests are still used as the training signal.

Initial metrics. Step-zero greedy evaluation on 593 rows had pass@1 0.3322 and test-pass fraction 0.4234. The first fresh 128-completion batch had mean hybrid reward 0.1882, test-pass fraction 0.2861, full-pass fraction 0.1563, partial-or-full completion fraction 0.4219, zero-reward fraction 0.5781, eight mixed reward groups, and DAPO surrogate delta -0.0000651. These stochastic rollout metrics are not directly comparable to the greedy step-zero evaluation. The first optimizer update completed in about 90 seconds without an out-of-memory error.

Runtime, identifiers, and environment. Trainer-reported runtime was 4,884.3388 seconds (81 minutes 24 seconds, about 97.7 seconds per optimizer update). The job completed its configured `max_steps: 50` normally and produced the final adapter and final metrics; this was not a process failure. W&B: https://wandb.ai/kunal-personal/grpo-mbpp/runs/gf4rxh0w. Config: `configs/grpo-mbpp-fulltrain-8x16-b128-micro2-workers8-0.5b-20260924.yaml`. Output directory: `outputs/grpo-mbpp-fulltrain-8x16-b128-micro2-workers8-0.5b-20260924`. Code commit: d91e9a3. Environment: one NVIDIA RTX 4000 Ada GPU with 20 GiB; TRL 0.25.0, Transformers 4.56.2, Accelerate 1.15.0, vLLM 0.10.2, and PyTorch 2.8.0+cu126. Model revision and dataset revision were not pinned beyond the identifiers above.

Completed results. The 50 sampled rollout means were not monotonic. Averaged by ten-update windows, per-update mean hybrid reward was 0.2411, 0.2701, 0.3187, 0.2182, and 0.2640. The corresponding pooled test-pass fractions were 0.3001, 0.3315, 0.3673, 0.2709, and 0.3282; full-pass fractions were 0.2242, 0.2516, 0.3047, 0.2023, and 0.2461. The rollout batch metrics therefore improved from the first to third window, fell sharply in updates 31–40, and partially recovered in updates 41–50. The first ten versus last ten updates differ by only +0.0229 reward, +0.0281 test-pass fraction, and +0.0219 full-pass fraction. These are fresh samples over different tasks, so batch-to-batch variation includes task and generation variation.

The scheduled in-sample greedy evaluation over all 593 training rows changed from step 0 to step 50 as follows: average test reward 0.4227 to 0.4458, pooled test-pass fraction 0.4234 to 0.4458, partial-or-full pass fraction 0.5143 to 0.5481, and pass@1 0.3322 to 0.3440. The separately saved final adapter evaluation reports average reward/test-pass fraction 0.4441 and pass@1 0.3491. This is a modest improvement, not a monotonic training-reward curve and not evidence of held-out generalization.

The fixed-rollout probability probe also weakens over training. Mean per-completion mean-token log-probability change across each update window was -0.00581, -0.00817, -0.00256, -0.00067, and +0.00021. In the first 30 updates the probe generally reduced likelihood for negative-advantage completions while slightly raising it for positive-advantage completions. In the last 20 updates these movements were close to zero, and the final ten-update window slightly increased negative-advantage likelihood on average. This suggests the realized update signal became very small, but the probe measures mean token log probability rather than the exact clipped, token-masked DAPO objective; it is diagnostic, not a proof of optimizer or objective correctness. The per-update DAPO surrogate delta itself is noisy and often near zero.

Limitations. This is one 50-update seed. The scheduled evaluations use the training rows, and held-out EvalPlus was not run. The sampled rollout batches contain only eight tasks per update and are sensitive to task difficulty. Model and dataset revisions were not pinned. No throughput benchmark was collected.
------------------------------------------------------------------------
