EXPERIMENT: Fixed-eight-task MBPP GRPO with 0.5B model, 8 groups x 16 fresh generations

Status. Running, first optimizer update complete. W&B: https://wandb.ai/kunal-personal/grpo-mbpp/runs/yhhv94fw. The launched configuration is committed as 17cac71 on `main`.

Purpose. Test whether fresh-batch reward variation and weak overfitting on full MBPP were caused by changing task composition. Train repeatedly on the same eight tasks while keeping 16 fresh generations per task and accumulating 128 completions per optimizer update.

Selection and evaluation. Select the first eight normalized MBPP rows remaining after EvalPlus task-ID exclusion, in dataset order with seed 42: task IDs 601, 609, 613, 617, 621, 625, 627, and 634. This selection does not inspect test outcomes. Evaluate greedily on these same eight training tasks at initialization and every ten optimizer steps. This is an in-sample overfit diagnostic, not a held-out benchmark. `select_best_checkpoint: false` keeps evaluation labels out of candidate selection; the fixed step-50 adapter is the scheduled artifact.

Configuration and command. Based on `configs/grpo-mbpp-fulltrain-8x16-b128-micro2-workers8-0.5b-20260924.yaml`, with `max_train_samples: 8` and unique output, trace, log, and W&B paths. It preserves Qwen/Qwen2.5-Coder-0.5B-Instruct, seed 42, LoRA rank 16 / alpha 32 / dropout 0.05, DAPO loss, linear learning-rate schedule from 1e-5, beta 0.01, hybrid reward coefficient 0.75, 16 generations per task, generation batch size 128, per-device microbatch 2, gradient accumulation 64, eight scoring workers, maximum prompt/completion lengths 512/2048, bf16, and 50 optimizer updates. Each generation batch contains the same eight task IDs with 16 fresh completions per task.

Command: `HF_HUB_ENABLE_HF_TRANSFER=0 accelerate launch --num_processes 1 train.py --config configs/grpo-mbpp-fixed8-8x16-b128-micro2-workers8-0.5b-20260924.yaml`.

Run identifiers. W&B run name: `grpo-mbpp-fixed8-8x16-b128-micro2-workers8-0.5b-20260924`. Output: `outputs/grpo-mbpp-fixed8-8x16-b128-micro2-workers8-0.5b-20260924`. Configuration: `configs/grpo-mbpp-fixed8-8x16-b128-micro2-workers8-0.5b-20260924.yaml`. Parent run: https://wandb.ai/kunal-personal/grpo-mbpp/runs/gf4rxh0w. Environment and code are recorded in the parent experiment above; source commit for this experiment is recorded after launch configuration is committed.

Sampling-mode behavior. This active process started from source commit 17cac71 and its unchanged configuration has `fixed_rollout_replay: false`, so it is using fresh sampling. The explicit `rollout_sampling_mode` interface was added after launch and applies only to future runs; the process and its loaded configuration were not changed.

Initial metrics after update 1. Step-zero in-sample greedy evaluation over the same eight tasks had pass@1 0.125 (1/8), test-pass fraction 0.2083, and average test reward 0.2083. The first fresh rollout batch contains exactly 128 completions, 16 for each task ID. Its mean hybrid reward was 0.1061, pooled test-pass fraction 0.1491 (55/369 tests), full-pass fraction 0.0938 (12/128 completions), partial-or-full fraction 0.2109, and all-zero fraction 0.7891. Six of eight groups had mixed rewards. The optimizer completed without OOM; DAPO surrogate delta was -0.000115, positive-advantage mean-token log-probability delta +0.000671, and negative-advantage delta -0.003437. This is one rollout and is not a trend. Continue tracking every 128-completion fresh-batch reward, pooled test-pass fraction, full-pass fraction, per-task group variability, DAPO surrogate delta, probability-direction metrics, learning rate, and greedy in-sample pass@1 / test-pass fraction at steps 0, 10, 20, 30, 40, and 50.

Limitations. A small fixed task set tests overfitting only. Greedy evaluation and reward use the same tasks/tests, so results do not establish generalization. Completion sampling remains stochastic.
------------------------------------------------------------------------

EXPERIMENT: Restarted fixed-eight-task MBPP GRPO with 50,000-step ceiling

Status. Started from the base model after stopping the prior fixed-eight run at step 28/50. The prior process had no resumable adapter or optimizer checkpoint, so its 28 optimizer updates could not be continued. The replacement run uses a new W&B run name and output directory.

Purpose. Continue the fixed-eight-task fresh-rollout overfit diagnostic without the short 50-step stopping limit. The main decision measure is whether greedy in-sample pass@1 and test-pass fraction rise on the same eight tasks while the fresh 128-completion batch reward, pooled test-pass fraction, and full-pass fraction are tracked each update. This is an in-sample training diagnostic, not a generalization benchmark.

Data and selection. Use the first eight normalized MBPP training rows remaining after EvalPlus task-ID exclusion, selected by dataset order with seed 42: task IDs 601, 609, 613, 617, 621, 625, 627, and 634. Selection does not inspect test outcomes.

Configuration. `configs/grpo-mbpp-fixed8-8x16-b128-micro2-workers8-0.5b-50000-20260924.yaml` uses Qwen/Qwen2.5-Coder-0.5B-Instruct; LoRA rank 16, alpha 32, dropout 0.05; DAPO loss; fresh sampling; 16 generations per task; eight tasks and 128 completions per optimizer update; microbatch 2 and gradient accumulation 64; learning rate 1e-5 with a linear schedule; beta 0.01; hybrid reward coefficient 0.75; eight reward-scoring workers; prompt/completion limits 512/2048; bf16; evaluation on the same eight tasks every ten updates; checkpoint selection disabled; and `max_steps: 50000`. The high ceiling is an operational cap, not a claim that 50,000 updates are needed.

Identifiers and reproducibility. Config: `configs/grpo-mbpp-fixed8-8x16-b128-micro2-workers8-0.5b-50000-20260924.yaml`. Run name and output directory use the same `grpo-mbpp-fixed8-8x16-b128-micro2-workers8-0.5b-50000-20260924` identifier. Parent run: https://wandb.ai/kunal-personal/grpo-mbpp/runs/yhhv94fw. Environment is the same 0.5B fixed-eight run environment recorded above, on one NVIDIA RTX 4000 Ada GPU. The prior run cost about 40 minutes for 28 updates, implying roughly 48 days for 50,000 updates at that rate; actual runtime may vary. Code/config commit and W&B run ID will be appended after launch.

Selection and evaluation boundary. The same eight training tasks provide the frequent overfit diagnostic. `select_best_checkpoint: false` keeps their correctness labels out of checkpoint selection. No held-out benchmark is used in this diagnostic.

Metrics. Pending. Record per-update sampled hybrid reward, pooled test-pass fraction, full-pass fraction, rolling means, the DAPO surrogate and probability-direction diagnostics, and in-sample greedy task metrics at step 0 and every ten updates.

Limitations. This experiment repeats only eight tasks and does not establish full-data learning or generalization. A 50,000-step cap corresponds to many weeks at the measured throughput; it is set to avoid stopping after a short arbitrary run, with manual monitoring and stopping still required.

EXPERIMENT: Full-data MBPP GRPO with 0.5B model, 8 groups x 16 generations, 50,000-step ceiling

Status. Running from the base model. It replaced the just-prepared fixed-eight 50,000-step run before that run initialized. W&B run ID: `fictfvaw`, URL: https://wandb.ai/kunal-personal/grpo-mbpp/runs/fictfvaw. Started 2026-09-24 17:46 UTC.

Purpose. Return to all eligible MBPP training rows while retaining 128 completions per optimizer update, fresh sampling, and the prior 0.5B GRPO configuration. The high max-step ceiling prevents an arbitrary 50-update stop; it is an operational cap and not a target number of updates.

Data and candidate selection. Uses all 593 normalized MBPP training rows remaining after EvalPlus task-ID exclusion. Training reward uses the task tests. The complete training-pool greedy evaluation runs every ten updates for diagnostics only. `select_best_checkpoint: false` prevents evaluation labels from choosing checkpoints. No held-out benchmark measurement is implied.

Configuration. `configs/grpo-mbpp-fulltrain-8x16-b128-micro2-workers8-0.5b-50000-20260924.yaml` preserves Qwen/Qwen2.5-Coder-0.5B-Instruct; seed 42; LoRA rank 16, alpha 32, dropout 0.05; DAPO loss; fresh sampling; 8 task groups x 16 generations; 128 completions per optimizer update; microbatch 2 and accumulation 64; learning rate 1e-5 with linear schedule; beta 0.01; hybrid reward coefficient 0.75; eight reward-scoring workers; prompt/completion lengths 512/2048; bf16; in-sample train-pool evaluation every ten steps; and `max_steps: 50000`.

Identifiers. Config, W&B run name, output directory, reward trace, probability trace, and logs use `grpo-mbpp-fulltrain-8x16-b128-micro2-workers8-0.5b-50000-20260924`. Code commit at launch: `bb4b107`. Prior comparable full-data run: https://wandb.ai/kunal-personal/grpo-mbpp/runs/gf4rxh0w.

Initial metrics. Step-zero greedy evaluation completed on all 593 tasks. These match the first update of the prior run from the same seed/config, as expected. Track the same per-update fresh rollout mean reward, pooled test-pass fraction, full-pass fraction, rolling means, DAPO surrogate and probability-direction diagnostics, and periodic in-sample pass@1 / test-pass fraction as the prior full-data run.

Runtime and limitations. The prior 50-step full-data run took 4,884 seconds, about 98 seconds per update. At that pace, 50,000 updates would take about 57 days. Runtime may change; monitor resource use and stop manually based on diagnostic results. This one-seed experiment does not establish generalization.

EXPERIMENT: Superseded dual-metric run using EvalPlus 0.2.1

Status. Manually stopped after ten optimizer updates while the step-10 evaluation was underway, because EvalPlus 0.2.1 uses an older MBPP+ release than the previous run. EXPERIMENT: Full-data MBPP GRPO dual greedy pass@1 run 1a612fxg, closure

Status. Stopped manually at step 960 after observing a train/evaluation divergence consistent with overfitting. W&B run `1a612fxg` was marked failed after the interruption; no further optimizer steps ran.

Final metrics. By step 960, `training/greedy_pass_at_1` had risen to 0.863 while `evaluation/greedy_pass_at_1` (canonical MBPP+) had fallen to 0.434, below its 0.437 step-zero baseline. The best logged evaluation was step 470: MBPP base pass@1 0.630 and MBPP+ pass@1 0.521 (MBPP+ tied at steps 350 and 360). No model weights were retained for step 470, because `select_best_checkpoint: false` and `save_strategy: "no"` deleted every intermediate evaluation snapshot; only an incidental step-970 adapter (49 MB) survived the interruption.

Diagnostic findings. A rollout-trace classification of all 124,160 generated training completions found the share of "lookup"-style solutions, branches that pattern-match the literal input values in the visible assertions instead of solving the task generally, rose from about 1% of full-pass rollouts in steps 1-100 to about 54% of full-pass rollouts in steps 850-969, while per-group solution diversity fell. A parallel classification of the official 378-task benchmark solutions found "visible-example specialization" in 0/378 solutions at step 0, 1/378 at step 470, and 91/378 at step 960. A code audit confirmed the training reward at this point graded only the same assertions shown in the prompt, with no held-out reward test, so a hardcoded lookup table for those exact assertions received full reward with no penalty for generalization failure. The audit also found no task-disjoint validation split existed to catch this before EvalPlus regressed, the 593-task training pool was resampled roughly 13 times by step 960, the learning-rate schedule had barely decayed (about 1.94%) by that point, and 3 of 593 training prompts exceeded `max_prompt_length: 512` and were silently truncated during training only, unrelated to the main overfitting mechanism. Full subagent reports from the 2026-09-27 analysis session are archived at `/tmp/overfit_outputs_report.txt`, `/tmp/overfit_code_audit.txt`, and `/tmp/overfit_rollouts_report.txt`; they were not copied into the repository.

Selection independence. These diagnostics used only logged training and evaluation metrics and completions already produced by the run. No ground truth beyond the existing MBPP and EvalPlus test oracles was introduced, and no selection decision beyond stopping the run was made from them.
------------------------------------------------------------------------

EXPERIMENT: Full-data MBPP GRPO with dual greedy pass@1, one visible assertion per training prompt

Status. Launching from the base model. W&B run ID and initial metrics will be appended after step zero.

Purpose. Test whether hiding reward-graded assertions from the training prompt reduces the visible-assertion memorization identified in the 1a612fxg closure above. Only the first of each training task's assertions is shown in the prompt; the reward function continues to grade the complete assertion list for that task, so a solution that only satisfies the single visible assertion no longer receives full reward.

Configuration change. Added a `visible_test_count` config key threaded through `data.py` (`build_qwen_evalplus_prompt`, `normalize_record`, `load_mbpp`, `prepare_datasets`) that truncates the prompt's assertion list to the first N entries. `format_tests`, which builds the reward-graded `test_code` field, is untouched and always uses the complete assertion list. The key defaults to unset, which shows all assertions and preserves prior behavior for every existing config. This run sets `visible_test_count: 1`. The EvalPlus evaluation dataset and canonical EvalPlus benchmark path are unaffected by this key; only MBPP training-split prompts are truncated.

Setup. Otherwise identical to the 1a612fxg run: Qwen/Qwen2.5-Coder-0.5B-Instruct; seed 42; LoRA rank 16, alpha 32, dropout 0.05; DAPO loss; learning rate 1e-5; beta 0.01; hybrid reward coefficient 0.75; 16 generations per task; 8 task groups and 128 completions per optimizer update; microbatch 2; gradient accumulation 64; eight reward workers; fresh sampling; bf16; max_steps 50000; EvalPlus 0.3.1; `select_best_checkpoint: false`; `evalplus_eval_steps: 10`.

Configuration. `configs/grpo-mbpp-fulltrain-8x16-b128-micro2-workers8-0.5b-50000-dual-greedy-evalplus031-visible1-20260927.yaml`.

Metrics. Same `training/greedy_pass_at_1` and `evaluation/greedy_pass_at_1` pair as the prior run, plus all existing rollout reward and optimizer diagnostics.

Selection independence. Checkpoint selection remains disabled by schedule (`select_best_checkpoint: false`); EvalPlus scores are logged for trend observation only and do not choose a checkpoint or stop the run.

Identifiers and environment. Code commit and W&B run ID will be appended after launch. Environment: one NVIDIA GeForce RTX 3090, PyTorch 2.8.0+cu128, EvalPlus 0.3.1.

Limitations. This is a single-factor, single-seed change. It does not by itself address the other contributing factors identified in the 1a612fxg closure: the missing validation split, task-pool resampling, the near-constant learning rate, or the 3-task prompt truncation bug.
------------------------------------------------------------------------

EXPERIMENT: 50-task hardcoding canary, code prepared but not launched

Status. Not launched. Code and config are committed and validated with a CPU-only dry run of `prepare_datasets`; no GPU job has been started. The concurrently running `rnnxompo` run (`visible_test_count: 1`) was not stopped, paused, or modified to prepare this.

Purpose. Test whether the reward-hacking mechanism found in the 1a612fxg closure above (per-task literal-assertion memorization) reproduces an order of magnitude faster on a much smaller training pool, so future overfitting checks do not require waiting to step ~850-970 as in that run. This is a mechanism-reproduction canary, not a quantitative forecast of when the phenomenon would occur in the full 593-task run.

Task selection and independence from ground truth. Recomputed the strict lookup-table classifier directly from 1a612fxg's saved `reward-trace.jsonl` (function called in the tests; >=2 parameter==literal comparisons matching actual test call arguments; >=2 literal returns; no calls, loops, assignments, try, or raise anywhere in the function). This is a structural check on the completions the model already generated during that run, not new ground-truth labeling: it does not execute or grade the code, only classifies its shape. Of the 593 training tasks, 317 were caught with a strict hardcoded solution at some point (earliest onset step 851, most within a roughly 120-step window ending at step 970), and 276 were never flagged. Selected 38 task IDs from the known-317 (positive control) and 12 from the known-276 (negative control) with `random.Random(42)`. Candidate selection used only the model's own generated completions and the existing MBPP/EvalPlus test oracles; no new external ground truth was introduced, and this selection does not feed back into or replace the eventual EvalPlus benchmark measurement, which stays independent.

Selected task IDs (50): 24, 42, 44, 48, 49, 117, 121, 176, 179, 184, 188, 193, 194, 204, 258, 288, 318, 321, 360, 376, 379, 383, 385, 387, 417, 461, 466, 496, 504, 509, 544, 634, 645, 647, 666, 683, 691, 701, 746, 761, 822, 874, 878, 909, 921, 952, 953, 961, 962, 972.

Configuration change. Added a `train_task_ids` config key in `data.py`'s `prepare_datasets`, which filters the training split to an explicit task-ID allowlist after the existing EvalPlus-exclusion filter; unset (the default) preserves every existing config's behavior unchanged. Added `is_hardcoded_lookup` and `hardcoding_rate` to `sandbox.py`, porting the same strict classifier used for the offline 1a612fxg analysis, and wired `hardcoding_rate` into `reward_function`'s diagnostics as `reward/hardcoding_rate`. This flows into W&B at every optimizer step through the existing `_reward_diagnostics` path in `train.py`; no changes to `train.py` were required. Re-validated the classifier against 1a612fxg's real trace after fixing a double-counting bug relative to the original ad-hoc analysis script: early-window rate 0.05%, peak-window 0.20%, late-window 27.65%, the same near-zero-then-sharp-rise story as the original analysis, with corrected absolute counts.

Setup. `visible_test_count` is deliberately left unset, matching 1a612fxg's all-visible regime, since the goal is to reproduce that known failure faster, not to test the `visible_test_count` fix on a small pool. Otherwise identical to 1a612fxg: Qwen/Qwen2.5-Coder-0.5B-Instruct; seed 42; LoRA rank 16, alpha 32, dropout 0.05; DAPO loss; learning rate 1e-5; beta 0.01; hybrid reward coefficient 0.75; 16 generations per task; 8 task groups and 128 completions per optimizer update; microbatch 2; gradient accumulation 64; eight reward workers; fresh sampling; bf16; max_steps 50000; EvalPlus 0.3.1; `select_best_checkpoint: false`. `evalplus_eval_steps` tightened from 10 to 5 for finer resolution given the much shorter expected run.

Configuration. `configs/grpo-mbpp-50task-hardcoding-canary-20260928.yaml`.

Metrics. `training/greedy_pass_at_1` and `evaluation/greedy_pass_at_1` as in prior runs, plus the new `reward/hardcoding_rate` diagnostic logged every optimizer step, alongside all existing rollout reward and optimizer diagnostics.

Selection independence. Checkpoint selection remains disabled by schedule (`select_best_checkpoint: false`); EvalPlus scores are logged for trend observation only and do not choose a checkpoint or stop the run.

Identifiers and environment. Code commit and W&B run ID will be appended once this is actually launched. Environment when launched is expected to match the current environment: one NVIDIA GeForce RTX 3090, PyTorch 2.8.0+cu128, EvalPlus 0.3.1.

Limitations. Shrinking the pool accelerates task-repetition count, the driver identified in the 1a612fxg closure, but it also collapses task diversity; any eval-score degradation in this run must be attributed causally via `reward/hardcoding_rate` (or offline reclassification) rather than trusted from the aggregate EvalPlus score alone, since a narrow-curriculum generalization effect unrelated to reward hacking could produce a superficially similar aggregate drop. Absolute step counts from this run are not expected to transfer quantitatively to the 593-task setting; only the qualitative existence and character of the mechanism is expected to transfer.
------------------------------------------------------------------------

EXPERIMENT: Visible-assertion verifier with static lookup filter

Purpose. Test whether the existing deterministic lookup-hardcoding detector improves selection after candidates pass the one visible MBPP assertion. Candidate selection sees only the visible assertion and completion source. Full-test outcomes are read from the existing reward trace only after selection, for offline measurement; hidden assertions are not executed by this experiment.

Run and checkpoint. Active W&B run `c4fthzd3`, using `configs/grpo-mbpp-fulltrain-8x16-b128-micro2-workers8-0.5b-50000-dual-greedy-evalplus031-visible1-save10-restart-20260929.yaml`. Latest saved checkpoint at measurement time was step 140. Its greedy training pass@1 was 0.36762 across 593 tasks and 0.36250 on the matched 80-task sample. Model: Qwen/Qwen2.5-Coder-0.5B-Instruct; seed 42; LoRA rank 16; DAPO; learning rate 1e-5; visible_test_count 1; 16 completions per task group; evaluation and save interval 10 steps.

Data and procedure. Took the latest 1,280 reward-trace rows at analysis time, comprising 16 candidates for each of 80 unique task IDs. Ran the first assertion from each task's test code against each candidate using `sandbox.execute_code`, sequentially at nice 19 and a one-second timeout. For the static arm, after a candidate passed that assertion, applied `sandbox.is_hardcoded_lookup(code, visible_assertion)` and skipped flagged candidates, selecting the first remaining candidate. For the visible-only arm, selected the first candidate passing the assertion. For the full-test oracle, selected any candidate in the subset with a recorded full-test pass. Evaluated the selected candidate's full-test status from the already-recorded `detail.status`, without rerunning hidden tests. For each k, averaged over all uniformly selected k-subsets of the 16 candidates, preserving generation order for first-match selection. The static detector received only the one visible assertion.

Results. Latest step-140 checkpoint greedy pass@1: 36.76% on all 593 tasks and 36.25% on the matched 80 tasks. On the 80-task rollout sample, full-test oracle pass@k was 33.52%, 41.85%, 50.10%, 56.96%, and 61.25% for k = 1, 2, 4, 8, and 16. Visible-assertion first-match selection measured against recorded full tests was 33.52%, 40.96%, 47.20%, 51.00%, and 52.50%. Adding the static lookup filter produced the same values: 33.52%, 40.96%, 47.20%, 51.00%, and 52.50%. Of 516 candidates that passed the visible assertion, the detector flagged zero; all 516 survived, and 429 had recorded full-test pass status.

Interpretation and limitations. The static filter did not improve this sample. It was applied only the one visible assertion, and its existing classifier requires at least two parameter-to-literal comparisons matching tested inputs and multiple literal returns; one visible example provides little evidence for detecting lookup code. Zero flags do not show absence of hard-coding. The rollout sample covers 80 tasks and candidates from a recent multi-step window, not 16 generations from one frozen checkpoint. Full-test labels were only used after selection for reporting; no hidden test was executed or inspected during selection. No LLM verifier or candidate-consensus experiment was run.

Identifiers and environment. Source commit `b4db7dca2bbae4886cae81f9d6d14c1ddaf1e28a`; W&B run `c4fthzd3`; checkpoint details `outputs/grpo-mbpp-fulltrain-8x16-b128-micro2-workers8-0.5b-50000-dual-greedy-evalplus031-visible1-save10-restart-20260929/training-step-140-details.json`; reward trace in the same output directory. GPU: NVIDIA RTX 2000 Ada Generation. Analysis used the existing `sandbox.py` implementation; no training code or model artifacts were changed.
------------------------------------------------------------------------

------------------------------------------------------------------------
EXPERIMENT: Hidden-input output clustering after visible-assertion filtering

Purpose. Test unsupervised answer clustering as a verifier reranker. The verifier first executes only the one visible assertion. For visible-passing candidates, it then calls each candidate on the input expression from each remaining MBPP assertion and records the returned values. It never reads or evaluates the expected output expressions. The resulting clusters are used to choose a candidate; existing full-test pass/fail labels are opened only after selection is finalized, to measure the selection strategies offline.

Run and baseline. W&B run `c4fthzd3`, config `configs/grpo-mbpp-fulltrain-8x16-b128-micro2-workers8-0.5b-50000-dual-greedy-evalplus031-visible1-save10-restart-20260929.yaml`, Qwen/Qwen2.5-Coder-0.5B-Instruct, seed 42, LoRA rank 16, DAPO, learning rate 1e-5, visible_test_count 1, 16 completions per prompt group. Latest saved checkpoint was step 150. Its greedy pass@1 was 0.38449 over all 593 training tasks and 0.38750 over the matched 80 rollout groups.

Data and answer logging. Used the latest 1,280 reward-trace rows as read at the start of the analysis: 80 prompt groups, 16 candidates each, 80 unique task IDs, and two hidden MBPP assertions per task. Candidate calls on hidden test inputs used only the left-hand function-call expression from each assertion; comparator/expected expressions were not read by the verifier or written to the answer log. Visible-passing candidates produced 1,164 logged hidden-input calls; 1,154 returned serializable answers and 10 raised errors or otherwise failed. 68 of 80 groups had at least one visible-passing candidate, and 67 had at least one candidate returning successfully on both hidden inputs. The largest exact two-output cluster averaged 89.3% of fully responding visible-passing candidates in groups where such responses existed. The logged answers are in ignored artifact `outputs/grpo-mbpp-fulltrain-8x16-b128-micro2-workers8-0.5b-50000-dual-greedy-evalplus031-visible1-save10-restart-20260929/consensus-hidden-outputs-step150-window.jsonl`; summary metrics are in `consensus-hidden-output-summary-step150.json`. These logs contain generated outputs and execution status, not expected answers.

Selection rules. For each k, averaged over all uniformly chosen k-subsets of the 16 candidates, preserving candidate order for first-match tie breaks. `visible` selects the first candidate in the subset passing the visible assertion. `static` additionally applies `sandbox.is_hardcoded_lookup` with only the visible assertion. `joint_cluster` selects the earliest candidate in the largest exact cluster of its two hidden-input outputs among visible passers who returned both outputs; if no candidate has both outputs, it falls back to the visible selection. `marginal_majority` computes a unique per-input modal output and selects the candidate matching the greatest number of those modes, with the same visible-selection fallback if there is no usable mode. The full-test oracle selects success whenever a candidate in the subset has a recorded full-test pass.

Results. Rows are k = 1, 2, 4, 8, 16. The matched checkpoint baseline pass@1 is 38.75%. Full-test oracle pass@k: 35.08%, 44.58%, 53.18%, 60.94%, 67.50%. Visible-first selection scored 35.08%, 42.96%, 48.72%, 52.76%, 56.25%. Visible plus static lookup filter scored the same 35.08%, 42.96%, 48.72%, 52.76%, 56.25%; the static detector again rejected zero visible-passing candidates. Joint two-output clustering scored 35.08%, 43.11%, 49.93%, 55.29%, 60.00%. Per-input modal-output ranking scored 35.08%, 43.11%, 49.88%, 55.17%, 60.00%.

Interpretation and limitations. Both consensus variants beat visible-first selection from k=2 onward, with the largest gain at k=16: 60.00% versus 56.25%, a 3.75 percentage-point improvement. The full-test oracle reached 67.50%, so clustering remained 7.50 points lower. High output agreement did not guarantee correctness: the most common two-output signature was often wrong according to the post-selection full-test measurement. Thus output consensus is a modest reranker, not a correctness verifier. This is one 80-group recent rollout window with candidates from a rolling training period, not 16 generations from one frozen checkpoint. Candidate calls used a 0.7-second timeout at nice 19, serially, and did not pause or modify training. Expected outputs were not used for selection or clustering; recorded full-test status was used only for the final offline score.

Identifiers and environment. Source commit `b4db7dca2bbae4886cae81f9d6d14c1ddaf1e28a`; W&B run `c4fthzd3`; step-150 details and reward trace under `outputs/grpo-mbpp-fulltrain-8x16-b128-micro2-workers8-0.5b-50000-dual-greedy-evalplus031-visible1-save10-restart-20260929/`. GPU: NVIDIA RTX 2000 Ada Generation. No training code or model artifacts were changed.
------------------------------------------------------------------------

Additional post-selection measurement for `EXPERIMENT: Hidden-input output clustering after visible-assertion filtering`. Matched the answer-log groups back to their exact 16-row blocks in the reward trace by task ID sequence. The largest joint output-signature cluster contained at least one full-test-correct candidate in 48/80 groups (60.0%). There was at least one full-test-correct candidate anywhere among the 16 in 54/80 groups (67.5%), so the largest-cluster correct-candidate recall was 48/54 = 88.9% conditional on the 16-sample set containing any correct candidate. The six missed oracle-solvable groups had a correct candidate outside the largest cluster. Full-test labels were consulted only for this post-selection coverage measurement; the clustering itself used only visible-pass status and candidate outputs on the two hidden inputs.

EXPERIMENT: Full-training-set verifier comparison from latest recorded rollout group per task

Purpose. Extend the baseline, full-test oracle, visible-assertion selector, and joint-output-cluster comparison from the 80-task sample to all 593 MBPP training tasks without generating additional model outputs or using the training GPU.

Run and baseline. W&B run `c4fthzd3`; latest saved checkpoint was step 180. Its greedy training pass@1 was 0.39292 over all 593 tasks. Configuration is `configs/grpo-mbpp-fulltrain-8x16-b128-micro2-workers8-0.5b-50000-dual-greedy-evalplus031-visible1-save10-restart-20260929.yaml`, with Qwen/Qwen2.5-Coder-0.5B-Instruct, seed 42, LoRA rank 16, DAPO, learning rate 1e-5, visible_test_count 1, and 16 generations per prompt.

Candidate selection and data. For each of the 593 tasks, used its most recent complete 16-completion group in the saved reward trace. The trace contained 1,656 prompt groups at analysis time. The latest chosen group was a median 296 prompt groups (about 37 optimizer updates) behind the newest group, the 90th-percentile lag was 802 groups (about 100 updates), and the 99th-percentile lag was 917 groups (about 115 updates). The trace does not record a model checkpoint ID per group, so these candidates do not all come from checkpoint 180. This is a full-task-set analysis of historical rollouts, not a same-checkpoint generation benchmark.

Verifier procedure. For each candidate, executed the visible assertion first. For visible-passing candidates, executed the candidate function on the input expressions from the two remaining MBPP assertions and logged returned outputs without reading their expected expressions. For each k in 1, 2, 4, 8, and 16, averaged over all uniformly selected k-subsets of each task's 16 candidates, preserving candidate order for first-match behavior. `visible` picks the first visible-passing candidate. `joint_cluster` selects the earliest candidate from the largest exact pair-of-outputs cluster among candidates with successful outputs on both hidden inputs, falling back to the visible selection if needed. The full-test oracle reports whether any candidate in the subset passed the full training tests. Full-test statuses were read only after selection to score the verifier. No hidden tests were checked against their expected outputs during verifier selection.

Results. Across all 593 tasks, checkpoint-180 greedy baseline pass@1 was 39.29%. Full-test oracle pass@k for k = 1, 2, 4, 8, 16 was 34.13%, 42.69%, 50.33%, 56.93%, and 62.06%. Visible-assertion selection scored 34.13%, 41.51%, 47.29%, 51.39%, and 53.46%. Joint-output clustering scored 34.13%, 41.60%, 48.22%, 53.28%, and 57.00%. At k=16, joint clustering improves 3.54 percentage points over visible-only selection and is 5.06 points below the oracle.

Limitations. The comparison spans all training tasks but uses each task's latest logged rollout group, with substantial and variable lag from the newest model state. The step-180 greedy baseline is therefore not a matched-policy baseline for every candidate group. At k=1, the stochastic rollout candidate pass rate is 34.13%, below the checkpoint greedy score of 39.29%; this is expected to reflect sampling and model-time mismatch, not a calculation error. A same-checkpoint full-set comparison would require generating 9,488 candidates from checkpoint 180 and would compete for GPU resources, so it was not performed.

Artifacts and environment. Hidden-input outputs are in ignored artifact `outputs/grpo-mbpp-fulltrain-8x16-b128-micro2-workers8-0.5b-50000-dual-greedy-evalplus031-visible1-save10-restart-20260929/consensus-hidden-outputs-fulltrain-step180-latestgroup.jsonl`; the summary is `consensus-hidden-output-fulltrain-summary-step180.json`. Source commit `b4db7dca2bbae4886cae81f9d6d14c1ddaf1e28a`; W&B run `c4fthzd3`; GPU: NVIDIA RTX 2000 Ada Generation. Candidate checks ran serially at nice 19, without GPU use or training interruption.
------------------------------------------------------------------------

# MBPP checkpoint evaluation comparison

Both selected checkpoints were sampled and evaluated on the same 378 EvalPlus MBPP tasks. The MBPP table scores the original base tests. The MBPP+ table scores the base and extra tests together. Each task has 16 temperature-sampled candidates.

## Checkpoint selection

All 131 saved checkpoint evaluation logs from W&B run `c4fthzd3` were scanned. The MBPP+ winner is step 630. The MBPP base-test winner is step 830. The MBPP values below use the saved greedy checkpoint logs only for checkpoint selection; the tables below are fresh 16-sample evaluations.

| Checkpoint | Selection basis | Saved MBPP pass@1 | Saved MBPP+ pass@1 |
|---|---|---:|---:|
| 630 | Highest saved MBPP+ greedy eval pass@1 | 64.0% | 54.0% |
| 830 | Highest saved MBPP greedy eval pass@1 | 65.1% | 53.2% |

## MBPP results

The verifier rows report expected accuracy over every uniformly selected subset of size k from the 16 candidates for each task. The raw row is the standard unbiased pass@k estimate.

| Checkpoint | Method | pass@1 | pass@2 | pass@4 | pass@8 | pass@16 |
|---:|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 630 | Raw sampling | 58.85% | 65.96% | 71.42% | 76.27% | 80.42% |
| 630 | Execution verifier | 58.85% | 64.86% | 69.11% | 72.69% | 75.40% |
| 630 | Execution verifier + joint output clustering | 58.85% | 64.87% | 69.20% | 72.70% | 76.19% |
| 830 | Raw sampling | 60.63% | 67.04% | 71.47% | 74.65% | 76.98% |
| 830 | Execution verifier | 60.63% | 65.97% | 69.40% | 71.64% | 72.22% |
| 830 | Execution verifier + joint output clustering | 60.63% | 66.01% | 69.59% | 71.84% | 73.02% |

## MBPP+ results

The verifier rows report expected accuracy over every uniformly selected subset of size k from the 16 candidates for each task. The raw row is the standard unbiased pass@k estimate.

| Checkpoint | Method | pass@1 | pass@2 | pass@4 | pass@8 | pass@16 |
|---:|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 630 | Raw sampling | 49.74% | 56.17% | 61.02% | 65.16% | 69.05% |
| 630 | Execution verifier | 49.74% | 53.98% | 56.69% | 58.61% | 59.52% |
| 630 | Execution verifier + joint output clustering | 49.74% | 53.98% | 56.82% | 58.79% | 60.05% |
| 830 | Raw sampling | 51.09% | 57.04% | 61.33% | 64.76% | 67.72% |
| 830 | Execution verifier | 51.09% | 55.21% | 57.80% | 59.43% | 60.05% |
| 830 | Execution verifier + joint output clustering | 51.09% | 55.22% | 57.92% | 59.51% | 60.32% |

## Setup and methodology

- Model: `Qwen/Qwen2.5-Coder-0.5B-Instruct`, with the LoRA adapter from run `c4fthzd3` at checkpoints 630 and 830.
- Sampling: vLLM 0.10.2, 16 candidates per task, temperature 1.0, top-p 1.0, seed 42, maximum 2,048 new tokens, using the repository EvalPlus prompt wrapped in Qwen ChatML.
- Dataset: EvalPlus 0.3.1 MBPP release, 378 tasks, dataset hash `ee43ecabebf20deef4bb776a405ac5b1`.
- Scoring: EvalPlus checked both base and extra tests. `MBPP` success means `base_status == pass`; `MBPP+` success means both base and plus status are pass. Early-exit test checking was enabled, with a 1-second minimum test limit, 4x ground-truth time factor, four workers, and a 2 GiB per-test-process memory limit.
- Execution verifier: test each candidate against the first visible assertion, then select the first visible-passing candidate in each subset, falling back to the earliest candidate if none passes.
- Joint output clustering: among visible-passing candidates, run the candidate on the remaining MBPP base-test inputs without reading expected outputs. Select the earliest candidate in a largest exact typed-output cluster; if no candidate yields a complete signature, fall back to execution selection.
- Selection separation: candidate verifier selections were fixed before full-test labels were read. MBPP labels were used only for the MBPP table; combined base-plus-extra labels were used only for the MBPP+ table.
- Selection caveat: each checkpoint was chosen using greedy pass@1 on the same benchmark family later used for reporting, so these checkpoint-comparison results have selection bias and are not an independent held-out estimate.
- Environment: Python 3.12, PyTorch 2.8.0+cu128, NVIDIA RTX 2000 Ada Generation with 16,380 MiB memory. Repository source commit `ba8b794085828aef55d617ac1d2b7a20dd2dee89`.
- Raw candidates, EvalPlus results, verifier selection counts and metrics, and the exact sampling and verifier scripts are in ignored artifact `outputs/mbpp-eval-best-checkpoints-20261003/`.

Experiment: `base-0.5b-sampled-evalplus-378-20261004`

Purpose. Measure the untrained 0.5B Instruct model with one temperature-sampled answer per task, so the blog can show a sampled baseline beside the reported greedy baseline and the post-trained sampling results.

Setup. Model: `Qwen/Qwen2.5-Coder-0.5B-Instruct`, without an adapter. Dataset: EvalPlus 0.3.1 MBPP, the cached 378-task split with dataset hash `ee43ecabebf20deef4bb776a405ac5b1`. Each task used the same Qwen ChatML prompt as the checkpoint comparison: system instruction `You are an intelligent programming assistant to produce Python algorithmic solutions`, followed by `Can you complete the following Python function?`, the task prompt in a Python code fence, then an assistant Python code fence. Generation used vLLM 0.10.2, bfloat16, one candidate per task, temperature 1.0, top-p 1.0, seed 42, maximum 2,048 new tokens, maximum model length 4,096, and the same stop strings as the checkpoint candidate run. The benchmark scorer used EvalPlus 0.3.1, four workers, early test exit, a 1-second minimum test limit, and a 4x ground-truth time limit factor.

Candidate artifact and scoring. Generated candidates are in ignored artifact `outputs/baseline-sampled-20261004/candidates.jsonl`; per-task EvalPlus inputs are in `outputs/baseline-sampled-20261004/mbpp/qwen2_chat_temp_1.0/`. Candidate generation completed for all 378 tasks before EvalPlus read correctness labels. There was one candidate per task, so no candidate selection or use of ground truth occurred before scoring. MBPP counts base-test passes. MBPP+ counts candidates that pass both base and extra tests.

Results. MBPP: 108 of 378 tasks passed, for 28.57% (28.6%). MBPP+: 84 of 378 passed, for 22.22% (22.2%).

Environment and reproducibility. NVIDIA RTX 2000 Ada Generation with 16 GB memory; PyTorch 2.8.0+cu128; vLLM 0.10.2; Python 3.12. Source repository HEAD at experiment launch: `4ea421d57247d72e25e31a664016a3bde8c4eceb`. The ignored generation script is `outputs/baseline-sampled-20261004/generate.py`, SHA-256 `08002389b20309e140d0fb2a2f8a89492685260eb66ef77f70f57da8c4578e8f`. Limitation: this is one sample per task with a fixed seed, so it measures one sampled decoding run and is not an estimate averaged over multiple seeds. The report's greedy baseline may use a different evaluation setup.

EXPERIMENT: Checkpoint 830 training-set generation replay and failure analysis

Purpose. Preserve the selected checkpoint's saved greedy training completions outside the transient run logs, execute each saved program on all its training assertions, capture assertion and exception details, and count recurrent failure shapes.

Run and data. W&B run `c4fthzd3`, name `grpo-mbpp-fulltrain-8x16-b128-micro2-workers8-0.5b-50000-dual-greedy-evalplus031-visible1-save10-restart-20260929`. Model `Qwen/Qwen2.5-Coder-0.5B-Instruct`, LoRA checkpoint 830, seed 42, rank 16, DAPO, learning rate 1e-5, one visible test in the prompt, 16 rollout generations per prompt group, hybrid reward coefficient 0.75. The original MBPP cache contains 374 train, 90 validation, and 500 test records. After the configured canonical EvalPlus task exclusion, the training pool has 593 tasks. Each checkpoint details row contains one greedy completion and three scorer assertions.

Artifacts and selection. `analysis/checkpoint_830/checkpoint_830_training_generations.jsonl` is a durable copy of all 593 saved checkpoint-830 greedy completions, their saved outcome/reward fields, and full scorer test code mapped from the reward trace. SHA-256: `1a6ea0564ada64eb51035112f3e43618d8edd7dfd577466fd77542bac2b35cce`. No new completions were generated. `analysis/checkpoint_830/checkpoint_830_error_analysis.json` contains each task, all 1,779 assertion outcomes, complete stderr and stdout for failures, actual and expected values when available, reference solution, task text, and pattern membership. SHA-256: `002fb4ab9620456292ad5475546fa8190d6fc27a7ee305914550fefe6d9c3f57`. Selection was not performed; these are the saved greedy completions. Correctness labels were used only for post-training analysis.

Procedure. Loaded task text and reference solutions from the local cached Hugging Face dataset in offline mode. Used `sandbox.split_test_cases` and `sandbox.execute_code` to run each assertion in a fresh isolated-mode Python subprocess and temporary directory, with a three-second timeout. Used eight worker threads. Failed direct comparisons received a second diagnostic execution that evaluated the actual and expected expressions once each and printed their representations. The four-task pilot ran 12 test cases in 0.244 seconds. The full replay covered 593 programs and 1,779 test cases plus 450 comparison diagnostics in 4.891 seconds. The replay command was `HF_DATASETS_OFFLINE=1 HF_HUB_OFFLINE=1 PYTHONPATH=. python /tmp/replay_checkpoint830.py`; driver SHA-256 at execution: `2010a0125e6b355e56911c36cca18f6eaadee78ca696ad984333eebf018f4b11`.

Results. The replay passed 1,281/1,779 test cases. There were 498 nonpassing cases, comprising 450 assertion mismatches, 45 runtime exceptions, and three syntax errors. At the program level, 372/593 passed all tests, 107 were partial (49 passed one test and 58 passed two), 113 passed none, and one was malformed. Every per-task pass count exactly matched `training-step-830-details.json`. The 450 assertion mismatches were numeric 224, string 79, sequence length 53, sequence content or shape 38, Boolean 23, sequence order 18, set 3, mapping 3, missing/None return 3, and other value mismatches 6. Runtime errors were IndexError 23, TypeError 7, NameError 6, ValueError 6, and re.error 3. Percentages and task membership are in the JSON analysis file.

Training/evaluation context. Exact local training greedy pass@1 values were 32.55% at step 0, 62.73% at step 830, and 70.32% at step 1300. The W&B MCP returned a 100-point sampled evaluation history with EvalPlus greedy pass@1 of 43.9% at step 0, a sampled peak of 54.0% at step 630, 53.2% at step 830, and 52.1% at step 1300. Thus training-pool pass@1 continued to rise after the sampled EvalPlus curve stopped improving; this association is not a causal test of dataset diversity.

W&B curve audit. Run history was retrieved from `kunal-personal/grpo-mbpp`, run `c4fthzd3`. A 50-point sample across internal steps 13,074–250,199 showed `reward/mean` from 0.207 to 0.833, `rewards/reward/std` from 0.350 to 0.497, `training/average_reward` from 0.279 to 0.527, `training/rolling_average_reward` from 0.306 to 0.701, `reward/flat_group_fraction` from 0 to 1, and `reward/mean_group_std` from 0 to 0.323. Sampled `reward/format/mean`, `reward/syntax/mean`, and `reward/interface/mean` were all zero; test-progress and pass components carried reward. Retrieval was sampled, not exhaustive. The exact local checkpoint metrics and complete assertion replay are the reported sources for checkpoint-830 results.

Environment and limitations. Python 3.12.3; 128 CPUs visible; eight replay workers; GPU unused. Sandbox source blob at the replay commit was `c26db028beeb3d2f8a89339108f1d076a958ccce`; repository HEAD was `755dc67e2214aa87f2e1c7efad880abf5affb8a4`. The local subprocess sandbox is not hostile-code isolation. A static AST scan found no obvious filesystem, network, shell, subprocess, or dynamic-evaluation calls in the archived completions. Three assertions per task are not exhaustive behavioral tests. W&B histories were sampled; exact target-step history retrieval was not available through MCP.

EXPERIMENT: mbpp-task-taxonomy-and-synthetic-generation-prompts-20261005

Purpose. Group the MBPP training and evaluation task descriptions into reusable program-task families. Create one synthetic-task generation prompt for each family, with three training examples and their tests. Estimate the token cost of each standalone prompt and their combined use.

Data and setup. The training source is the 593-task checkpoint-830 archive from W&B run `c4fthzd3`, `grpo-mbpp-fulltrain-8x16-b128-micro2-workers8-0.5b-50000-dual-greedy-evalplus031-visible1-save10-restart-20260929`, using Qwen/Qwen2.5-Coder-0.5B-Instruct. Task text is in `analysis/checkpoint_830/checkpoint_830_error_analysis.json`; saved tests are in `analysis/checkpoint_830/checkpoint_830_training_generations.jsonl`. The evaluation source is the canonical `get_mbpp_plus()` task set from EvalPlus 0.3.1, MBPP+ v0.2.0, with 378 task IDs. The older local `qwen_eval/eval_plus/MbppPlus-v0.1.0.jsonl` has 399 rows and was not used for this comparison.

Deduplication and selection. The 593 archived training task IDs have no ID overlap with the 378 evaluation IDs. A normalized task-description comparison found two exact cross-ID duplicates: training ID 217 matches evaluation ID Mbpp/602, and training ID 928 matches Mbpp/427. These two tasks are excluded from the effective taxonomy and prompt-example pool, leaving 591 training tasks. The historical checkpoint archive remains unchanged. `data.py` now removes both shared IDs and matching normalized task descriptions during future dataset preparation. Task labels and example selection use task wording and IDs only. The model's generated solutions and correctness results did not affect the labels or example selection.

Taxonomy method. Assign each task one primary type based on the requested result or central algorithm. This makes the counts mutually exclusive. Category boundaries remain analyst judgments. The 13 categories are arithmetic and numeric formulas; number properties and number theory; lists and sequence transformations; search, sorting, and selection; counting and frequency; strings and text processing; set and membership operations; mappings, tuples, and record aggregation; recurrence, combinatorics, and dynamic programming; matrices and grids; geometry and spatial calculations; graphs, paths, intervals, and chains; and predicates, validation, and classification.

Prompt artifact. `analysis/mbpp_task_taxonomy_and_generation_prompts.txt` contains the category definitions, counts and shares, 13 standalone generation prompts, and three training examples with three tests for each prompt. The 39 example task IDs are 136, 452, 491, 48, 148, 107, 41, 729, 154, 152, 200, 189, 40, 700, 13, 15, 621, 27, 22, 249, 431, 26, 613, 81, 169, 291, 248, 231, 642, 314, 51, 180, 76, 110, 660, 846, 552, 910, and 950.

Environment and identifiers. EvalPlus version: 0.3.1. Token estimator: `tiktoken` 0.14.0, `cl100k_base`. Dataset filter change was committed as `389938b` before this report was written. No model generation, training, or benchmark evaluation was run for this analysis. The task taxonomy is based on analyst-assigned primary labels and is not an error-category or correctness analysis.

Limitations. Token counts are estimates for a proxy tokenizer and may differ from the Qwen tokenizer. Task types overlap in practice, but this report forces one primary label per task to provide a count distribution. The archived model run used all 593 training tasks, including the two descriptions identified as cross-ID duplicates; removing them from the historical archive would change the record rather than the training data already used.

EXPERIMENT: mbpp-category-synthetic-preview-qwen05-20261006
============================================================================
Purpose: Measure one greedy base-Qwen completion on one generated synthetic task from each of the 13 taxonomy categories. This is a preview of base-model solvability. It is not a synthetic-training experiment and does not measure EvalPlus improvement.

Source and setup:
- Taxonomy: `analysis/mbpp_task_taxonomy_10x.json`, with 591 task labels and category counts.
- Prompt generation code: `analysis/generate_mbpp_category_prompts.py`; run with `--prompts-per-category 1 --seed 20261006` to create 13 prompts in `analysis/mbpp_category_prompt_preview.jsonl`.
- Each prompt contains three distinct examples from the same category. The prompt IDs and example task IDs are saved with the prompts.
- Generation model: GPT-6 Luna, invoked through three parallel subagents. One response was retained for each fixed prompt. The agent interface did not expose a sampling temperature or model revision.
- Generated specifications: `analysis/mbpp_category_synthetic_task_specs.jsonl`. Each contains a task description, reference function, and exactly three literal Python call inputs. The agents did not supply expected outputs.
- Ground-truth procedure: `analysis/materialize_mbpp_category_tasks.py` ran each call against the reference function using `sandbox.execute_code`, with a 5-second timeout. It derived expected outputs and assertions and saved them in `analysis/mbpp_category_synthetic_tasks_preview.jsonl`.
- Candidate model: base `Qwen/Qwen2.5-Coder-0.5B-Instruct`, Hugging Face revision `ea3f2471cf1b1f0db85067f1ef93848e38e88c25`. No fine-tuned checkpoint or adapter was loaded.
- Candidate generation: repository EvalPlus ChatML prompt, vLLM 0.10.2, bfloat16, greedy temperature 0, top-p 1, maximum 2,048 new tokens, with the repository Qwen stop strings. One sample was generated per task.
- Candidate scoring: `sandbox.score_completion`, with a 3-second timeout per assertion. No candidate selection was performed. Ground-truth outputs were used to construct the assertions before model scoring.
- Code and artifact commit: `59b90f9` (`Generate category preview tasks and run base Qwen`). Taxonomy source commit: `869699a`. Prompt generator and runbook commit: `32b574d`.
- Environment: Python 3.12.3, PyTorch 2.8.0+cu128, Transformers 4.56.2, vLLM 0.10.2, one NVIDIA A40 GPU. A fresh Hub cache was used because the pre-existing model cache had empty files.

Results:
- Task pass@1: 7/13 = 53.8462%.
- Assertion pass fraction: 27/39 = 69.2308%.
- Per-category results and raw completions are saved in `analysis/mbpp_category_synthetic_qwen05_eval.json`.

| Category | Prompt | Example task IDs | Qwen assertions passed | Task passed |
| --- | --- | --- | ---: | --- |

| Number properties and number theory | 02-001 | 483, 855, 295 | 3/3 | yes |
| Lists and sequence transformations | 03-001 | 883, 815, 345 | 1/3 | no |
| Search, sorting, and selection | 04-001 | 970, 37, 209 | 3/3 | yes |
| Counting and frequency | 05-001 | 13, 384, 258 | 1/3 | no |
| Strings and text processing | 06-001 | 186, 584, 877 | 3/3 | yes |

Limitations: This is one generated task and three generated input cases per category. The results are sensitive to these small samples. The assertions come from the generated reference functions and are not independent hidden tests. The pass rates describe only these 13 tasks. They do not show that training on these tasks will improve held-out MBPP or EvalPlus performance. A controlled training comparison and untouched held-out evaluation are required for that claim.

The outcomes were 1,111 full passes (26.3%), 2,105 partial passes (49.8%), 905 zero-test passes (21.4%), and 107 syntax errors (2.5%). Interface validation failed for 448/4,228 completions (10.6%); this is an overlapping diagnostic, not an additional status category. One syntax-error sample emitted `return person_hours: {}`. One wrong-algorithm sample checked whether input values themselves increase instead of checking whether consecutive differences increase. One partial sample passed 2/3 assertions.

The local per-step log has 137 metric rows through step 136. Cumulative mean reward decreased from 0.4375 at step 1 to 0.3751 at step 136; the mean over the last ten logged steps was 0.3748. Mean test-pass fraction was 0.4778 over the first ten steps and 0.3861 over the last ten; full-pass fraction was 0.250 and 0.225, respectively. The model still received nonzero gradients (last logged norm 1.669) and parameter updates (last logged update norm 0.00123), with no NaN or crash observed. These findings indicate weak/noisy reward improvement with frequent incomplete and incorrect programs, rather than a stopped optimizer. The raw sample logger reports missing code fences as format errors, but the scoring path wraps continuations before parsing, so this message alone does not establish a zero training reward.

Trend calculations above use the local per-step log. The first due canonical EvalPlus evaluation is step 140. Checkpoint selection remains disabled.

Comparison with W&B run `c4thzd3`, 2026-10-06. The linked run used the same Qwen 0.5B base model, seed 42, LoRA rank 16, DAPO loss, and 2,048-token completion limit. Its config used learning rate 1e-5, 16 generations per task, beta 0.01, hybrid reward coefficient 0.75, and `visible_test_count: 1`. The current synthetic pilot uses learning rate 1e-6, four generations per task, beta 0, reward coefficient 0.5, and no `visible_test_count` limit. Both use a 512-token prompt limit and three scorer assertions per task. The linked run trained on 593 original MBPP tasks; the pilot trains on 100 synthetic tasks.

The linked run's prompt exposed one of its three assertions while reward scoring used all three. The synthetic loader builds prompts from all three saved synthetic assertions and scores those same three, so it has no assertion held out from the prompt. `synthetic_tests_enabled: false` disables an additional test-augmentation path; it does not remove the three assertions stored with each synthetic task. Thus the pilot has a scalar reward on three assertions, but lacks the linked run's hidden-assertion signal. Its four-sample per-task GRPO comparison also has fewer candidate alternatives than the linked run's 16-sample group. The reward formula gives full completion weight 0.5 here versus 0.75 in `c4thzd3`.

Metric interpretation. Local greedy training-pool evaluation for `c4thzd3` improved from average reward 0.413 and pass@1 0.325 at step 0 to average reward 0.791 and pass@1 0.703 at step 1,300. Those are greedy evaluations on 593 tasks. The pilot's `training/average_reward` is instead the cumulative mean of sampled rollout rewards, not a greedy evaluation; it was 0.438 at step 1 and 0.375 at step 136 before the restart. A direct comparison of these values is invalid. The current config now schedules training-pool greedy evaluation and EvalPlus every 10 steps, so step 140 will provide the first resumed evaluation, but there is no step-zero baseline for this run.

Resume logging limitation. The callback initializes cumulative and rolling reward counters to zero and does not restore them from the checkpoint. Attempt 2 therefore restarted `training/average_reward` at about 0.448 around trainer step 131. This is a logging discontinuity, not a model improvement. The prior pre-restart cumulative value at step 136 was about 0.375. Exact comparison of cumulative reward across supervisor restarts is not supported by the current callback implementation.

============================================================================
EXPERIMENT: Synthetic-task difficulty, repetition, and EvalPlus transfer audit

Purpose. Analyze the saved 10x Luna task artifact, checkpoint greedy evaluations, and GRPO rollout trace to distinguish easier synthetic tasks, task-distribution mismatch, and task-specific overfitting as explanations for the EvalPlus plateau. This is a post-hoc analysis of existing artifacts. No model generation, training, or benchmark scoring was run.

Run and sources. W&B run `mbpp10xluna1007`, run name `grpo-mbpp-fulltrain-8x16-b128-micro2-workers8-0.5b-50000-dual-greedy-evalplus031-visible1-save10-10x-luna-20261007`. The mixed training pool has 591 original tasks and 5,319 Luna synthetic tasks. Sources are `analysis/mbpp_category_synthetic_tasks_api10x.jsonl`, `analysis/mbpp_task_taxonomy_10x.json`, `outputs/grpo-mbpp-fulltrain-8x16-b128-micro2-workers8-0.5b-50000-dual-greedy-evalplus031-visible1-save10-10x-luna-20261007/training-step-{0,100,300,400,500,600,660}-details.json`, the corresponding training metrics, `reward-trace.jsonl`, and EvalPlus step results through 660. The reward trace contains 85,632 completion rows across 669 optimizer updates.

Split checkpoint results. The same step-zero model passed all three training assertions on 32.3% of original tasks and 47.0% of synthetic tasks. Mean task reward was 0.410 on originals and 0.675 on synthetics. At step 660, original pass@1 was 43.8% with mean reward 0.517; synthetic pass@1 was 80.7% with mean reward 0.892. Thus both groups improved, while synthetic performance improved much more. The combined training mean reward was 0.854 at step 660, and 90% of rows in that aggregate are synthetic. Each group has three assertions per task, so the gap is not explained by a higher test count for synthetic rows.

Rollout trend. Across the first 100 optimizer steps, sampled mean reward was 0.327 on original completions and 0.440 on synthetic completions. Across steps 501-669 it was 0.400 on original completions and 0.761 on synthetic completions. These are sampled rollout statistics over different task groups, not paired task-level measurements. The per-checkpoint greedy measurements above provide the stronger comparison.

EvalPlus trend. Greedy pass@1 changed from 53.2% MBPP and 44.7% MBPP+ at step zero to 61.1% and 49.7% at step 660. From step 400 to 660, MBPP+ was 49.7% at both endpoints. MBPP changed from 59.5% to 61.1%, with an intermediate high of 61.9%. This supports a late plateau, but the 378-task benchmark is small and the trend alone does not identify a cause.

Task difficulty and distribution signals. Median task-description length was 78 characters for the 591 original tasks and 264 for the synthetics. A simple keyword scan found explicit constraint or edge-case wording in 6.4% of original descriptions and 92.4% of synthetic descriptions; this heuristic is only a wording measure. Median reference-solution AST size was similar, 43 nodes for original tasks and 50 for synthetics, so the higher synthetic pass rate does not establish lower algorithmic complexity. The synthetic pool has 1,207 distinct entry-point names, but it concentrates on recurring task patterns: `count_positive_divisors` occurs 156 times, `count_staircase_ways` 131 times, `collapse_whitespace` 93 times, `is_valid_time` 91 times, and `make_slug` 79 times. The top 20 entry-point names cover 24.7% of synthetic rows. Case-folding descriptions and replacing punctuation with spaces found six internally repeated description classes (12 rows), including repeated divisor-count and every-kth-element specifications. No normalized exact description overlapped the original task descriptions.

Generation repetition and lookup checks. After whitespace normalization, 592 synthetic greedy completions at step zero and 288 at step 660 duplicated a completion assigned to another synthetic task ID; the largest repeated completion group fell from 26 tasks to 17. At step 660, only two original-task completions were duplicated across task IDs. This is consistent with repeated canonical task families, but identical code can also be correct for distinct tasks. The repository's conservative literal-lookup classifier flagged zero checkpoint completions in both groups at steps zero and 660. This classifier misses many possible forms of overfitting and does not rule out template learning or memorization.

Interpretation. The strongest evidence favors an easier and more repetitive synthetic objective that transfers only partly to EvalPlus. The synthetic descriptions are much more explicit, the base model already scores them better, and their pass rate and reward rise much faster. Broad task categories mirror the original pool, so the evidence does not support a major shift in broad programming domains. The wording style and the concentration on recurring task patterns do differ. The trace does not show a collapse in within-task completion variety, and original-task performance improves rather than degrades. It therefore does not establish harmful instance-level overfitting. Over-specialization to the synthetic task templates remains plausible because no held-out synthetic validation set exists.

Limitations and next test. Synthetic references and their three tests were generated together and passed sandbox execution; this checks internal execution, not independent task validity or test coverage. Entry-point repetition and identical completions are indicators, not a semantic-deduplication audit. A controlled follow-up should hold optimizer updates and original-task exposure fixed, reduce the synthetic sampling share, and evaluate both EvalPlus and a separately generated, task-disjoint synthetic validation set. This would separate data difficulty, mixture weighting, and transfer to the generator's task style.

Environment and identifiers. The run's source commit was unavailable in its `run-results.txt`; the local artifacts identify W&B run `mbpp10xluna1007`. EvalPlus set: canonical 378-task MBPP+ v0.2.0. Training and evaluation code were not modified for this audit.

============================================================================
EXPERIMENT: 100 harder-than-example synthetic MBPP tasks and base-model evaluation

Purpose. Generate 100 category-proportional synthetic tasks that are explicitly required to be harder than their three same-category MBPP examples. Measure base Qwen 0.5B pass rate on the generated tasks using the same repository EvalPlus-style prompt and sandbox scoring path as a control evaluation on the earlier 100-task API set.

Task generation. Prompt input `analysis/mbpp_category_api_prompts_harder100.jsonl` contains 100 prompts allocated by largest remainders from the 591-task taxonomy using seed 20261009. Each prompt asks for greater algorithmic or reasoning demands and rejects longer wording or arbitrary restrictions as sufficient difficulty. Three original same-category examples are included. GPT-6 Luna with reasoning effort `none`, 20 concurrent requests, JSON output, and three retries generated task specifications. The generator rejected duplicate descriptions, validated one plain reference function per task, ran each reference against three literal calls in the subprocess sandbox, and materialized the expected-output assertions. All 100 tasks passed reference validation. API usage, including the two-task sanity sample and retries, was 129 responses, 87,074 input tokens, and 79,809 output tokens. Estimated cost was $0.048278. The generated task specs, prompts, materialized tasks, and usage ledger are saved under `analysis/mbpp_category_*_harder100*`.

Difficulty and duplication audit. Median description length is 586.5 characters versus 263 for the earlier easy API100 control. Median reference AST size is 164.5 nodes versus 53.0. These are rough complexity signals, not independent difficulty ratings. The 100 harder descriptions are unique under lowercase and punctuation normalization, with no exact normalized description overlap with the earlier control. The harder set has 88 distinct entry points; its most common entry point occurs four times. The two datasets use the same 13-category allocation but different seeds and sampled examples.

Evaluation method. Generated one greedy completion per task with base `Qwen/Qwen2.5-Coder-0.5B-Instruct`, with no adapter, temperature 0, top-p 1, and maximum 2,048 new tokens. Prompt construction used the repository Qwen EvalPlus ChatML wrapper, the task text, and the exact reference function signature; generated assertions were not shown. `sandbox.score_completion` scored each completion against all three materialized assertions with a 3-second timeout. The assertion denominator includes all 300 supplied assertions, including tests that were not executable after syntax-error completions. No candidate selection occurred. The matched easy control is `analysis/mbpp_category_synthetic_tasks_api100.jsonl`, evaluated with the same script and settings. This is an EvalPlus-style prompt and sandbox test of generated tasks, not the official 378-task EvalPlus benchmark.

Results. The harder set had 6/100 fully passing tasks, for greedy task pass@1 of 6.0%. The model passed 67/300 assertions, for an assertion pass fraction of 22.3%. Statuses were 6 passed, 44 partial, 47 failed, and 3 syntax errors. The matched easy API100 control had 45/100 fully passing tasks (45.0%) and passed 196/300 assertions (65.3%). The harder set was 39.0 percentage points lower in full-task pass rate and 43.0 points lower in assertion pass fraction. Category-level counts are in the per-task evaluation JSON.

Interpretation. The prompt change produced a substantially harder set for this base model under the measured greedy protocol. The 44 partial tasks show that many completions still received nonzero assertion-level feedback, but this one-completion-per-task evaluation does not show whether 16-sample GRPO groups will have useful within-group reward variation. Longer specifications and larger references may contribute to difficulty alongside algorithmic demands. Low pass rate alone does not establish that the tasks are unambiguous or independently valid.

Limitations and identifiers. Each generated oracle and its three tests came from the same task-generation response. Sandbox validation proves that the reference runs on those calls, not that task semantics or test coverage are independently correct. This is one 100-task draw, one seed, and one greedy completion per task. Model revision was not pinned. Prompt-generator change commit `6c1ee31`; prompt artifact commit `774994a`; task-generation artifact commit `febde5c`; evaluator assertion-denominator correction commit `5431272`. Evaluation used Python 3.12.3, PyTorch 2.8.0+cu128, vLLM 0.10.2, and one NVIDIA RTX A5000 with 24,564 MiB. Evaluation results are `analysis/mbpp_category_synthetic_harder100_qwen05_eval.json` and matched-control results are `analysis/mbpp_category_synthetic_api100_qwen05_eval_matched.json`.

============================================================================
EXPERIMENT: Sixteen-sample evaluation on the first twenty harder synthetic tasks

Purpose. Measure whether repeated base-model samples produce useful GRPO-style reward variation on the first twenty rows of the new harder-than-example synthetic set. Classify each task as none, all, or mixed according to whether zero, all sixteen, or one to fifteen completions pass all three assertions.

Setup. Input is `analysis/mbpp_category_synthetic_tasks_harder100.jsonl`; tasks were selected as the first twenty rows in file order, with no ground-truth-based selection. The model was base `Qwen/Qwen2.5-Coder-0.5B-Instruct`, with no adapter. Each task received sixteen samples using temperature 1.0, top-p 1.0, seed 42, and 2,048 maximum new tokens. Prompts used the repository Qwen EvalPlus ChatML wrapper and exact reference function signature. Scoring used the isolated `sandbox.score_completion` path with a 3-second timeout and all three saved assertions. A full pass requires all three assertions to pass. The assertion denominator counts all expected assertions, including assertions skipped after a syntax error. The evaluation script and full 320 completions with per-completion scores are saved in `analysis/evaluate_mbpp_harder20_16gen.py` and `analysis/mbpp_category_synthetic_harder20_16gen_eval.json`.

Results. Of 20 task groups, 13 (65%) had no completion pass all assertions, zero (0%) had all sixteen completions pass, and 7 (35%) had mixed outcomes. There were 11 full-pass completions among 320 (3.44%). Across 960 expected assertions, 157 passed (16.35%). Mean task-level assertion fraction was also 16.35%. The groups with at least one passing completion show that some tasks are solvable by this base model under sampling. The 13 all-zero groups indicate that most of these tasks provided no full-pass success in a group of sixteen, although some still provided partial assertion reward.

Task mix and limits. The first twenty rows cover six arithmetic tasks, thirteen number-theory tasks, and one list task. This is a prefix of a category-proportional dataset, not a representative sample of all thirteen categories. The model is the base model rather than the trained checkpoint. The evaluation uses generated tests and is EvalPlus-style, not the official EvalPlus benchmark. The task, reference, and tests were produced together, and sandbox-valid references do not establish independent semantic validity or test coverage. A single sampling seed and twenty tasks do not support a precise estimate of all synthetic-task group reward distributions.

Environment. Python 3.12.3, PyTorch 2.8.0+cu128, vLLM 0.10.2, NVIDIA RTX A5000 with 24,112 MiB reported memory. Evaluation code and result were produced from source commit `27a7ea389f6189364f4b9b479e01f641db04e25d`; the recorded experiment artifacts are committed separately.

Reward variation detail. Sixteen of the twenty groups had more than one distinct assertion-pass count among their sixteen completions, and sixteen groups had at least one completion pass at least one assertion. Thus, the 13 groups with no complete solution are not all flat zero-reward groups under assertion-fraction scoring. Four groups had no assertion pass in any completion. This is a test-progress signal only; the experiment did not calculate the configured GRPO hybrid reward or inspect optimizer advantages.

============================================================================
ANALYSIS: Saved sixteen-generation groups on the first twenty original MBPP training tasks

Purpose. Compare per-task assertion-pass coverage on original MBPP training tasks using generations already logged by the successful `c4fthzd3` GRPO run. No new generations were sampled.

Task selection. The first twenty records in the repository's original MBPP training pool after the canonical EvalPlus exclusion were selected in dataset order. Their MBPP task IDs are 601, 609, 613, 617, 621, 625, 627, 634, 636, 640, 642, 645, 646, 647, 648, 649, 650, 651, 652, and 653. The local cached original MBPP Arrow splits and the installed EvalPlus task IDs and descriptions reproduced the repository's 591-task filtered pool. Each selected task has three assertions.

Candidate selection and scoring. For each task, used its earliest complete sixteen-candidate group in `outputs/grpo-mbpp-fulltrain-8x16-b128-micro2-workers8-0.5b-50000-dual-greedy-evalplus031-visible1-save10-restart-20260929/reward-trace.jsonl`. The group was selected by first occurrence in the trace, without inspecting pass outcomes. The logged `detail.passed_tests` field reports the count of the three original assertions passed by each completion. These generations came from the evolving GRPO run, not a fresh fixed-checkpoint base-model evaluation. The source config showed one visible assertion per prompt and scored all three assertions. First-occurrence trace row offsets ranged from 560 to 10,352, so selected groups span early training updates. The source run is W&B run `c4fthzd3`; the original training pool contained 593 tasks.

Results. Counting whether at least one of sixteen completions passed exactly each number of tests: exactly zero, 19/20 tasks (95%); exactly one, 7/20 (35%); exactly two, 6/20 (30%); exactly three, 11/20 (55%). These rows overlap because a task can have completions with different scores. By cumulative threshold, at least one completion passed at least one assertion on 15/20 tasks (75%), at least two assertions on 15/20 (75%), and all three assertions on 11/20 (55%). The detailed per-task candidate histograms, in task selection order, were: 601 {0:16}; 609 {0:11,1:4,2:1}; 613 {0:15,3:1}; 617 {0:10,1:4,2:2}; 621 {0:15,3:1}; 625 {0:2,3:14}; 627 {0:7,1:5,2:1,3:3}; 634 {0:13,3:3}; 636 {0:1,1:9,2:6}; 640 {0:16}; 642 {0:16}; 645 {0:11,3:5}; 646 {0:8,1:4,2:4}; 647 {0:16}; 648 {0:15,3:1}; 649 {0:4,3:12}; 650 {1:1,2:14,3:1}; 651 {0:5,1:9,3:2}; 652 {0:16}; 653 {0:5,3:11}.

Limitations. This is not an apples-to-apples fixed-model comparison with the harder synthetic evaluation. The original generations used the run's one-visible-assertion prompt and came from different points in an evolving training run; the harder synthetic generations used the base model with no visible assertions. The original task IDs and assertion labels were not used to select candidates, but the run's generations were generated as training rollouts. These twenty ordered tasks are a small, domain-skewed slice of the original pool. Source run code commit was `b4db7dca2bbae4886cae81f9d6d14c1ddaf1e28a`; the saved local reward trace is the candidate artifact.
